<a id='question-001'></a>

### Question 001: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-01 (Blood Cell Disorders & Lymphoid)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular pathogenesis and characteristic cytogenetic abnormalities of specific lymphoid malignancies, particularly distinguishing between Burkitt lymphoma and other high-grade B-cell lymphomas based on genetic translocation and clinical presentation. (理解特定淋巴恶性肿瘤的分子发病机制和特征性细胞遗传学异常，特别是根据基因易位和临床表现区分伯基特淋巴瘤和其他高级别B细胞淋巴瘤。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old male from Equatorial Africa presents with a rapidly enlarging abdominal mass and generalized lymphadenopathy. He reports intermittent fever, night sweats, and recent weight loss (10% in 2 months). Physical examination reveals a palpable, non-tender mass in the right lower quadrant, hepatosplenomegaly, and widespread cervical and axillary lymphadenopathy. Laboratory studies show elevated serum lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) at 850 U/L (normal range: 100-250 U/L), hyperuricemia (serum uric acid 9.5 mg/dL, normal range: 3.5-7.0 mg/dL), and anemia (hemoglobin 9.0 g/dL, normal range: 13.5-17.5 g/dL). A biopsy of the abdominal mass shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are positive for CD20, CD10, and BCL6, and negative for CD5 and BCL2. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation involving the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14.

What is the most likely underlying genetic translocation responsible for this patient's condition?

(A) t(11;14)(q13;q32)
(B) t(8;14)(q24;q32)
(C) t(14;18)(q32;q21)
(D) t(14;22)(q32;q11)
(E) t(2;5)(p12;q35)

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 35-year-old male from Equatorial Africa. This geographic location is endemic for certain lymphomas, notably Burkitt lymphoma, often associated with Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection.
- **Symptoms:** Rapidly enlarging abdominal mass, generalized lymphadenopathy, fever, night sweats, weight loss. These are classic "B symptoms" associated with aggressive lymphomas. The rapid growth suggests a high-grade malignancy.
- **Physical Exam:** Palpable abdominal mass, hepatosplenomegaly, widespread lymphadenopathy. Consistent with systemic lymphoma involvement.
- **Labs:** Elevated LDH (marker of high cell turnover/tumor burden), hyperuricemia (due to rapid cell lysis releasing purines), anemia (bone marrow involvement or chronic disease). These findings support an aggressive, high-turnover malignancy.
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm ("starry sky" appearance is often seen but not explicitly mentioned here). This morphology is characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunophenotype:** CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, CD5-, BCL2-. This profile is typical for a germinal center B-cell lymphoma. CD10+ and BCL6+ are characteristic of germinal center origin. CD5- helps exclude mantle cell lymphoma and chronic lymphocytic leukemia/small lymphocytic lymphoma. BCL2- is crucial, as Burkitt lymphoma typically lacks BCL2 expression (unlike follicular lymphoma).
- **Cytogenetics:** Translocation involving the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14 (IgH locus at 14q32). This is a hallmark of many B-cell lymphomas.

The combination of rapid growth, endemic location, specific morphology, germinal center B-cell immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL6+), lack of BCL2 expression, and a translocation involving 14q32 strongly points towards Burkitt lymphoma. The most common translocation in Burkitt lymphoma involves the MYC oncogene on chromosome 8 and the IgH locus on chromosome 14.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Burkitt Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma. Its pathogenesis involves a chromosomal translocation that juxtaposes the MYC oncogene (located at 8q24) with one of the immunoglobulin (Ig) gene loci, most commonly the IgH locus (14q32). This translocation leads to overexpression of the MYC protein, a transcription factor that regulates cell growth, proliferation, apoptosis, and metabolism. Overexpression of MYC drives uncontrolled cell proliferation, characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Specific Translocations:**
    - t(8;14)(q24;q32): MYC gene on chromosome 8 is translocated to the IgH locus on chromosome 14. This is the most common translocation (~80% of cases).
    - t(2;8)(p12;q24): MYC gene on chromosome 8 is translocated to the kappa light chain locus on chromosome 2.
    - t(8;22)(q24;q11): MYC gene on chromosome 8 is translocated to the lambda light chain locus on chromosome 22.
- **Clinical Correlation:** The rapid proliferation driven by MYC overexpression explains the aggressive clinical course, high LDH levels (due to high cell turnover), and risk of tumor lysis syndrome (leading to hyperuricemia). The endemic form in Africa is strongly associated with EBV infection, which likely contributes to B-cell immortalization and proliferation, setting the stage for the MYC translocation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) t(11;14)(q13;q32): This translocation involves the CCND1 gene (encoding Cyclin D1) on chromosome 11 and the IgH locus on chromosome 14. It is the characteristic translocation found in Mantle Cell Lymphoma. Mantle cell lymphoma typically presents with lymphadenopathy and bone marrow involvement, often in older adults, and expresses CD5. The immunophenotype (CD5+) and the specific gene involved (CCND1) rule this out.
- (B) t(8;14)(q24;q32): This translocation involves the MYC gene on chromosome 8 and the IgH locus on chromosome 14. This is the most common genetic abnormality in Burkitt lymphoma, leading to MYC overexpression and uncontrolled proliferation. The clinical presentation (rapidly growing mass, B symptoms, endemic location), morphology, immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2-), and involvement of the IgH locus (14q32) are all highly consistent with Burkitt lymphoma caused by this translocation.
- (C) t(14;18)(q32;q21): This translocation involves the IgH locus on chromosome 14 and the BCL2 gene on chromosome 18. It is the hallmark of Follicular Lymphoma. Follicular lymphoma is typically an indolent lymphoma, although it can transform into a more aggressive form. It is characterized by BCL2 overexpression, which inhibits apoptosis, leading to the accumulation of long-lived B cells. The immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+) and indolent course are inconsistent with the rapidly progressive disease described in the vignette.
- (D) t(14;22)(q32;q11): This translocation involves the IgH locus on chromosome 14 and the minor breakpoint cluster region (MCR) gene on chromosome 22. This is the characteristic translocation found in Marginal Zone Lymphoma (specifically, the extranodal type often associated with MALT lymphoma). Marginal zone lymphomas are typically indolent B-cell lymphomas. The clinical presentation and immunophenotype are inconsistent with the vignette.
- (E) t(2;5)(p12;q35): This translocation involves the kappa light chain locus on chromosome 2 and the Ig alpha locus on chromosome 5. It is associated with a subset of B-cell acute lymphoblastic leukemia (B-ALL) and some B-cell lymphomas, but it is not the characteristic translocation for Burkitt lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (t(8;14)) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(11;14)) | Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18)) | Marginal Zone Lymphoma (t(11;18) or t(14;22)) |
|----------------------|---------------------------|---------------------------------|---------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------|
| **Translocation**   | t(8;14)(q24;q32)          | t(11;14)(q13;q32)               | t(14;18)(q32;q21)               | t(11;18)(q21;q21) or t(14;22)(q32;q11)          |
| **Gene Involved**    | MYC                       | CCND1 (Cyclin D1)               | BCL2                            | Various (e.g., MALT1, API2, IGH)                 |
| **Clinical Course**  | Very Aggressive           | Indolent/Aggressive              | Indolent                         | Indolent                                        |
| **Morphology**       | "Starry Sky"              | Small lymphocytes, irregular nuclei | Follicular pattern               | Variable, often resembling reactive lymphoid tissue |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2-        | CD5+, Cyclin D1+                 | CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+               | Variable, often CD19+, CD20+, CD79a+             |
| **LDH**              | High                      | Variable                        | Normal/Slightly Elevated         | Normal/Slightly Elevated                         |
| **Common Location**  | Abdomen, Jaw (endemic)    | Lymph nodes, BM, spleen          | Lymph nodes                      | MALT sites (stomach, spleen, salivary glands)    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is a high-grade B-cell lymphoma characterized by MYC overexpression due to translocations involving chromosome 8 (MYC) and an immunoglobulin locus (most commonly 14q32). Key features include rapid growth, endemic association (EBV), specific morphology, CD10+/BCL6+/BCL2- immunophenotype, and high LDH. Differentiating it from other lymphomas relies on recognizing these characteristic features, especially the specific translocation and immunophenotype. Remember t(14;18) is for Follicular Lymphoma (BCL2+), t(11;14) is for Mantle Cell Lymphoma (CD5+, Cyclin D1+), and t(14;22) is for Marginal Zone Lymphoma.

<a id='question-002'></a>

### Question 002: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-02 (and MyeloidNeoplasms)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the specific genetic translocation associated with Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and its downstream molecular consequences, specifically the constitutively active tyrosine kinase activity. (理解慢性髓性白血病 (CML) 相关的特定基因易位及其下游分子后果，特别是具有持续活性的酪氨酸激酶活性。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and early satiety for the past 3 months. He denies fever, weight loss, or bone pain. Physical examination reveals mild splenomegaly. A complete blood count (CBC) shows a markedly elevated white blood cell (WBC) count of 180,000/µL (normal range: 4,000-11,000/µL) with a left shift, including immature granulocytes (myelocytes, metamyelocytes, bands, and blasts). The red blood cell count and platelet count are within normal limits. A bone marrow aspirate and biopsy are performed. The bone marrow aspirate shows hypercellularity with myeloid hyperplasia, and the biopsy confirms this finding. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the presence of the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome). Which of the following molecular mechanisms is most directly responsible for the pathogenesis of this patient's condition?

(A) Fusion of the *BCR* gene on chromosome 22 with the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8, leading to overexpression of MYC.
(B) Fusion of the *BCR* gene on chromosome 22 with the *ABL1* gene on chromosome 9, resulting in a constitutively active tyrosine kinase.
(C) Translocation between chromosomes 15 and 17, creating the *PML-RARA* fusion gene, which blocks myeloid differentiation.
(D) Point mutation in the *JAK2* gene (V617F), leading to constitutive activation of the JAK-STAT signaling pathway and erythrocytosis.
(E) Overexpression of the *BCL2* gene due to translocation t(14;18), inhibiting apoptosis and leading to lymphocytosis.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 52-year-old male with fatigue, night sweats, early satiety (suggestive of splenomegaly), and mild splenomegaly on exam. These are classic symptoms of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML).
- **Laboratory Findings:** Markedly elevated WBC count (180,000/µL) with a left shift (presence of immature granulocytes). This indicates a proliferation of myeloid cells. Normal RBC and platelet counts are typical in the chronic phase of CML, although they can be affected in later phases.
- **Bone Marrow Findings:** Hypercellularity with myeloid hyperplasia confirms the overproduction of myeloid cells.
- **Cytogenetic Analysis:** Presence of the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome). This is the hallmark cytogenetic abnormality of CML.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular mechanism responsible for the pathogenesis.

The combination of clinical symptoms, marked leukocytosis with a left shift, splenomegaly, and the presence of the Philadelphia chromosome strongly points to CML. The Philadelphia chromosome results from a reciprocal translocation between the long arms of chromosome 9 and chromosome 22, denoted as t(9;22)(q34;q11). This translocation creates the *BCR-ABL1* fusion gene. The *BCR* gene encodes a GTPase, and the *ABL1* gene encodes a non-receptor tyrosine kinase. The fusion protein BCR-ABL1 possesses constitutive tyrosine kinase activity, meaning it is always "on" and does not require normal regulatory signals. This uncontrolled kinase activity drives the excessive proliferation of myeloid cells characteristic of CML.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Philadelphia Chromosome (Ph):** Result of t(9;22)(q34;q11).
- **Gene Fusion:** Creates the *BCR-ABL1* fusion gene on the derivative chromosome 22 (Ph chromosome).
- **Protein Product:** BCR-ABL1 fusion protein.
- **Molecular Mechanism:** The ABL1 kinase domain is constitutively active due to its fusion with the BCR protein. This leads to uncontrolled phosphorylation of downstream signaling molecules involved in cell growth, proliferation, differentiation, and survival. Key pathways activated include RAS/MAPK and PI3K/AKT/mTOR.
- **Consequence:** Unregulated proliferation of myeloid progenitor cells, leading to leukocytosis, splenomegaly, and potential progression to blast crisis.
- **Therapeutic Target:** The constitutively active tyrosine kinase activity of BCR-ABL1 is the target for tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs) like imatinib, dasatinib, and nilotinib, which are the mainstay of CML treatment.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Fusion of *BCR* with *MYC* is incorrect. The *BCR* gene fuses with *ABL1* in CML. *MYC* is involved in Burkitt lymphoma, typically associated with t(8;14) or variants involving the *MYC* locus on chromosome 8. Overexpression of MYC drives cell proliferation.
- (C): Translocation t(15;17) creating the *PML-RARA* fusion gene is characteristic of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML). The PML-RARA fusion protein disrupts myeloid differentiation, leading to an accumulation of promyelocytes. This patient's presentation (chronic symptoms, marked leukocytosis with a full spectrum of myeloid maturation, Ph chromosome) is inconsistent with APL.
- (D): Point mutation in *JAK2* (V617F) is characteristic of Polycythemia Vera (PV), Essential Thrombocythemia (ET), and Primary Myelofibrosis (PMF), which are myeloproliferative neoplasms (MPNs). The JAK2 V617F mutation leads to constitutive activation of the JAK-STAT pathway, primarily affecting erythropoiesis (PV), megakaryopoiesis (ET), or leading to fibrosis (PMF). This patient has marked leukocytosis, not erythrocytosis or thrombocytosis, and has the Ph chromosome, not a JAK2 mutation.
- (E): Overexpression of *BCL2* due to t(14;18) is characteristic of Follicular Lymphoma. The *BCL2* gene encodes an anti-apoptotic protein. Its overexpression inhibits apoptosis of B lymphocytes, leading to their accumulation. This patient has a myeloid proliferation, not a lymphoid one, and the cytogenetic abnormality is t(9;22), not t(14;18).

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) | Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL) | Polycythemia Vera (PV) | Follicular Lymphoma | Burkitt Lymphoma |
|----------------------|---------------------------------|------------------------------------|-------------------------|----------------------|-------------------|
| **Key Genetic Abnormality** | t(9;22) -> BCR-ABL1            | t(15;17) -> PML-RARA               | JAK2 V617F              | t(14;18) -> BCL2     | t(8;14) -> MYC    |
| **Cell Line Affected** | Myeloid                         | Myeloid (Promyelocytes)            | Myeloid (Erythroid)     | Lymphoid (B-cells)   | Lymphoid (B-cells)|
| **Hallmark Cell**    | Myelocytes, Metamyelocytes      | Promyelocytes                      | Erythrocytes            | Centrocytes, Centroblasts | Lymphoblasts      |
| **Typical Presentation** | Chronic symptoms, leukocytosis | Acute symptoms, DIC               | Erythrocytosis, pruritus | Lymphadenopathy      | Rapidly growing mass |
| **Molecular Mechanism** | Constitutive Tyrosine Kinase   | Blocked Differentiation            | Constitutive JAK-STAT   | Anti-apoptosis       | Overproliferation  |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The Philadelphia chromosome (t(9;22)) resulting in the BCR-ABL1 fusion protein with constitutive tyrosine kinase activity is the defining molecular abnormality of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML). This mechanism drives the uncontrolled proliferation of myeloid cells. Key differentials to consider based on genetic abnormalities include t(15;17) for APL, t(14;18) for Follicular Lymphoma, and JAK2 mutations for PV/ET/PMF. Remember the specific genes involved and the resulting protein function in each condition.

<a id='question-003'></a>

### Question 003: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-03 (LEARNINGOBJECTIVES)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the cytogenetic abnormality (t(14;18)) and its molecular consequence (IgH-BCL2 fusion protein) leading to constitutive anti-apoptotic signaling in Follicular Lymphoma, differentiating it from other lymphomas with different translocations or molecular mechanisms. (理解t(14;18)染色体易位及其分子后果(IgH-BCL2融合蛋白)在滤泡性淋巴瘤中的作用，并将其与其他具有不同易位或分子机制的淋巴瘤区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old woman presents to her primary care physician with painless swelling in her neck and axillae. She denies fever, night sweats, or weight loss (B symptoms). Physical examination reveals multiple, firm, non-tender lymph nodes in the cervical and axillary regions, ranging from 1 to 3 cm in diameter. Her spleen is palpable 3 cm below the left costal margin. Complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell count of 8,500/μL (normal range: 4,000-11,000/μL) with a normal differential. Serum lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) is mildly elevated at 280 U/L (normal range: 120-250 U/L). An excisional lymph node biopsy is performed. Histopathology shows effacement of the normal lymph node architecture by a proliferation of small to medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, vesicular chromatin, and inconspicuous nucleoli. The cells are arranged in a follicular growth pattern, forming nodules separated by thin septa containing a network of capillaries (creating a "fishnet" appearance). Immunohistochemistry reveals the neoplastic cells are positive for CD10, CD19, CD20, and BCL2, and negative for CD5 and BCL6. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a t(14;18)(q32;q21) translocation. Which of the following molecular events is the direct consequence of this cytogenetic abnormality and contributes most significantly to the pathogenesis of this lymphoma?

(A) Overexpression of the MYC oncogene due to its translocation to the IgH locus.
(B) Constitutive activation of the NF-κB signaling pathway due to the involvement of the REL gene.
(C) Formation of a fusion protein that inhibits apoptosis by sequestering pro-apoptotic proteins.
(D) Dysregulation of cell cycle control due to the translocation of the CCND1 gene to the IgH locus.
(E) Increased production of interleukin-2 (IL-2) due to the translocation of the IL2 gene to the IgH locus.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old woman with painless lymphadenopathy (neck, axillae) and splenomegaly, without B symptoms. This presentation is typical for indolent lymphomas.
*   **Lab Findings:** Normal WBC count, mildly elevated LDH. Normal WBC count is common in follicular lymphoma, unlike some other lymphomas (e.g., CLL, hairy cell leukemia). Elevated LDH can indicate tumor burden or cell turnover.
*   **Histopathology:** Effacement of lymph node architecture, follicular growth pattern ("fishnet" appearance), small to medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, vesicular chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli. This morphology is characteristic of follicular lymphoma.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** Positive for CD10, CD19, CD20 (B-cell markers), and BCL2. Negative for CD5 (rules out CLL/SLL) and BCL6 (typically positive in germinal center B-cells, but often negative in follicular lymphoma due to the t(14;18) translocation). The strong positivity for BCL2 is a key feature.
*   **Cytogenetics:** t(14;18)(q32;q21). This is the hallmark cytogenetic abnormality of follicular lymphoma.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the direct molecular consequence of the t(14;18) translocation contributing to pathogenesis.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   The t(14;18)(q32;q21) translocation juxtaposes the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) locus on chromosome 14 with the BCL2 gene on chromosome 18.
*   The IgH locus is highly active in B-cells, undergoing V(D)J recombination and high transcription rates.
*   This translocation leads to the constitutive overexpression of the BCL2 protein.
*   BCL2 is an anti-apoptotic protein. It functions by inhibiting the release of cytochrome c from mitochondria, thereby preventing the activation of caspases and blocking the intrinsic apoptotic pathway.
*   In normal B-cells, BCL2 expression is tightly regulated. In follicular lymphoma, the t(14;18) translocation leads to its overexpression, preventing apoptosis of germinal center B-cells that have acquired other mutations. This allows these cells to accumulate and form the characteristic follicular lymphoma.
*   Therefore, the direct molecular consequence is the formation of a fusion protein (or simply overexpression driven by the IgH enhancer) that inhibits apoptosis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) Overexpression of the MYC oncogene due to its translocation to the IgH locus.** This describes the t(8;14)(q24;q32) translocation, which is characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma. MYC is a transcription factor involved in cell proliferation and apoptosis. Its overexpression leads to uncontrolled cell growth. This is incorrect for follicular lymphoma.
*   **(B) Constitutive activation of the NF-κB signaling pathway due to the involvement of the REL gene.** This describes the t(11;14)(q13;q32) translocation, characteristic of MALT lymphoma. The REL gene encodes a subunit of NF-κB. Its translocation to the IgH locus leads to constitutive NF-κB activation, promoting cell survival and proliferation. This is incorrect for follicular lymphoma.
*   **(D) Dysregulation of cell cycle control due to the translocation of the CCND1 gene to the IgH locus.** This describes the t(11;14)(q13;q32) translocation, characteristic of Mantle Cell Lymphoma. The CCND1 gene encodes cyclin D1. Its overexpression drives cell cycle progression. This is incorrect for follicular lymphoma.
*   **(E) Increased production of interleukin-2 (IL-2) due to the translocation of the IL2 gene to the IgH locus.** While IL-2 is important for T-cell proliferation and function, and some lymphomas can produce cytokines, the t(14;18) translocation does not involve the IL2 gene. The key molecular event in follicular lymphoma is related to BCL2 and apoptosis inhibition. This is incorrect.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature                 | Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18)) | Burkitt Lymphoma (t(8;14)) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(11;14)) | MALT Lymphoma (t(11;14)) |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------- | :-------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :----------------------- |
| **Key Translocation**   | t(14;18)                       | t(8;14)                     | t(11;14)                       | t(11;14)                 |
| **Genes Involved**      | IgH-BCL2                      | IgH-MYC                    | IgH-CCND1                      | IgH-REL                 |
| **Molecular Consequence** | BCL2 overexpression, Anti-apoptosis | MYC overexpression, Proliferation | Cyclin D1 overexpression, Cell cycle dysregulation | NF-κB activation, Survival |
| **Morphology**          | Follicular pattern, CD10+, BCL2+ | Starry sky, Diffuse, CD10- | Diffuse/Nodular, CD5+, Cyclin D1+ | Variable, Often CD79a+ |
| **Clinical Course**     | Indolent                      | Aggressive                 | Indolent/Aggressive            | Indolent                 |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The t(14;18) translocation in follicular lymphoma leads to overexpression of the BCL2 anti-apoptotic protein, preventing programmed cell death and contributing to the accumulation of neoplastic B-cells. Remember to differentiate this translocation and its consequence (BCL2 overexpression) from other common lymphoma translocations involving the IgH locus, such as t(8;14) (MYC in Burkitt lymphoma), t(11;14) (CCND1 in Mantle Cell Lymphoma), and t(11;14) (REL in MALT lymphoma). Each translocation results in a distinct molecular abnormality driving lymphoma pathogenesis.

<a id='question-004'></a>

### Question 004: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-04 (❏Explain informationrelated to reactive changes in)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating reactive lymphoid hyperplasia from neoplastic processes like lymphoma, specifically focusing on the characteristic features of Hodgkin lymphoma (HL) and its variants, including the Reed-Sternberg (RS) cell morphology and immunohistochemical profile. (区分反应性淋巴组织增生与淋巴瘤等恶性过程，特别是霍奇金淋巴瘤 (HL) 及其变种的特征，包括 Reed-Sternberg (RS) 细胞的形态学和免疫组织化学谱。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 28-year-old female presents to her primary care physician with a painless, enlarging mass in her left supraclavicular region, noted over the past 3 months. She denies fever, night sweats, or unintentional weight loss (B symptoms). Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender, rubbery lymph node measuring approximately 4 cm in diameter. The remainder of the physical exam is unremarkable. Excisional biopsy of the lymph node is performed. Histopathological examination shows effacement of the normal lymph node architecture by a mixed inflammatory infiltrate consisting of lymphocytes, plasma cells, eosinophils, and histiocytes. Scattered large, atypical cells are present within this background. These atypical cells are binucleated or multinucleated, with prominent eosinophilic nucleoli, resembling "owl's eyes". Immunohistochemical staining reveals that these large atypical cells are positive for CD30 and CD15, but negative for CD45 (leukocyte common antigen) and most B-cell markers (CD20, PAX5).

What is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Reactive lymphadenopathy secondary to viral infection
(B) Diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL)
(C) Classical Hodgkin lymphoma, nodular sclerosis subtype
(D) Anaplastic large cell lymphoma (ALCL)
(E) Burkitt lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 28-year-old female. Young adult age group is common for certain lymphomas, particularly Hodgkin lymphoma.
- **Presentation:** Painless, enlarging supraclavicular lymph node (4 cm) over 3 months. This suggests a localized lymphoproliferative process. Absence of B symptoms (fever, night sweats, weight loss) is noted, but their absence doesn't rule out lymphoma.
- **Histopathology:** Effacement of normal architecture by a mixed inflammatory infiltrate (lymphocytes, plasma cells, eosinophils, histiocytes). This mixed cellular background is characteristic of classical Hodgkin lymphoma (cHL).
- **Atypical Cells:** Presence of scattered large, atypical cells. These are the key diagnostic cells.
- **Morphology:** Binucleated or multinucleated cells with prominent eosinophilic nucleoli ("owl's eyes"). This is the classic description of Reed-Sternberg (RS) cells, pathognomonic for Hodgkin lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** RS cells are positive for CD30 and CD15, and negative for CD45 and B-cell markers (CD20, PAX5). This immunophenotype is highly specific for cHL. CD45 negativity distinguishes RS cells from most reactive lymphocytes and other lymphoma cells. CD30 and CD15 positivity, along with B-cell marker negativity (despite B-cell origin), is characteristic of cHL.
- **Diagnosis:** Based on the clinical presentation, histopathology showing a mixed inflammatory background with characteristic RS cells, and the specific immunophenotype (CD30+, CD15+, CD45-, CD20-), the diagnosis is classical Hodgkin lymphoma. The nodular sclerosis subtype is the most common subtype, often presenting in young adults with cervical or supraclavicular lymphadenopathy.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Hodgkin Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** cHL is characterized by malignant RS cells (derived from germinal center B cells) surrounded by a reactive inflammatory microenvironment. The RS cells themselves are relatively sparse (often <1% of the total cells in the node).
- **RS Cell Origin:** RS cells are clonal B cells that have lost expression of most B-cell markers (CD20, PAX5) but retain some B-cell lineage markers (like CD30, CD15, HL61). They also fail to express CD45, a pan-leukocyte marker.
- **Microenvironment:** The reactive inflammatory infiltrate (lymphocytes, eosinophils, plasma cells, histiocytes) plays a crucial role in the pathogenesis and clinical presentation of cHL. These cells are recruited by cytokines secreted by the RS cells and contribute to the symptoms and disease progression.
- **Subtypes:** cHL has several subtypes: nodular sclerosis (most common, often presents in young adults with mediastinal/cervical nodes, characterized by fibrous bands dividing the node into nodules), mixed cellularity (more common in older adults, B symptoms more frequent), lymphocyte-rich (good prognosis), and lymphocyte-depleted (rare, aggressive). The vignette description (young adult, supraclavicular node, mixed infiltrate) is most consistent with nodular sclerosis.
- **Molecular Basis:** EBV infection is associated with a subset of cHL cases. Genetic alterations in RS cells include mutations in genes involved in NF-κB signaling (e.g., REL, NFKBIA), JAK/STAT pathway (e.g., SOCS1), and immune checkpoints (e.g., PD-L1/PD-L2).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Reactive lymphadenopathy secondary to viral infection:** Reactive hyperplasia typically shows preserved lymph node architecture (follicles, sinuses), a polyclonal lymphocyte population, and lacks the characteristic RS cells with the specific immunophenotype (CD30+, CD15+, CD45-). While viral infections can cause lymphadenopathy, the presence of distinct, large atypical cells with "owl's eye" nucleoli and the specific IHC profile rules this out.
- (B) **Diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL):** DLBCL is a common non-Hodgkin lymphoma (NHL). It typically presents as a rapidly enlarging mass. Histologically, it shows diffuse effacement of the node by sheets of large, atypical B lymphocytes. These cells are usually positive for B-cell markers (CD20, PAX5) and CD45, and negative for CD15. The presence of RS cells and the specific CD30+/CD15+/CD45-/CD20- phenotype excludes DLBCL.
- (D) **Anaplastic large cell lymphoma (ALCL):** ALCL is a T-cell lymphoma (or null-cell type). The large atypical cells in ALCL are typically positive for CD30 but also express T-cell markers (like CD3) or lack both T-cell and B-cell markers (null-cell). They are usually CD15 negative and CD45 positive. The morphology (often kidney-shaped or horseshoe nuclei, less prominent nucleoli than RS cells) and immunophenotype differ significantly from RS cells.
- (E) **Burkitt lymphoma:** Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell lymphoma characterized by monotonous proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, and prominent nucleoli. It typically shows a "starry sky" pattern due to interspersed macrophages. The cells are positive for B-cell markers (CD20, PAX5) and CD45, and negative for CD15. The morphology and immunophenotype are completely different from RS cells.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Classical Hodgkin Lymphoma (cHL) | Reactive Lymphadenopathy | DLBCL                 | ALCL                 | Burkitt Lymphoma       |
|----------------------|-----------------------------------|---------------------------|-----------------------|----------------------|------------------------|
| **Key Cell**        | Reed-Sternberg (RS) cell          | Polyclonal lymphocytes   | Large B-cells         | Large atypical cells | Medium-sized B-cells  |
| **RS Cell Morphology**| Binucleate/Multinucleate, "Owl's eye" | N/A                       | Variable, large       | Variable, large      | Monotonous, medium    |
| **Background**      | Mixed inflammatory infiltrate     | Polyclonal infiltrate    | Sheets of large cells | Sheets of large cells| Sheets of medium cells|
| **CD30**             | Positive (RS cells)               | Negative                  | Variable (often neg) | Positive             | Negative               |
| **CD15**             | Positive (RS cells)               | Negative                  | Negative              | Negative             | Negative               |
| **CD45**             | Negative (RS cells)               | Positive                  | Positive              | Positive             | Positive               |
| **CD20**             | Negative (RS cells)               | Positive                  | Positive              | Variable (often neg) | Positive               |
| **B-cell Markers**   | Negative (RS cells)               | Positive                  | Positive              | Variable             | Positive               |
| **Architecture**     | Effaced                           | Preserved (usually)       | Effaced               | Effaced              | Effaced                |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The key to diagnosing classical Hodgkin lymphoma is recognizing the characteristic Reed-Sternberg cells within a mixed inflammatory background and confirming their specific immunophenotype (CD30+, CD15+, CD45-, CD20-). This distinguishes it from reactive hyperplasia, other lymphomas like DLBCL and ALCL, and Burkitt lymphoma, which have different morphologies and immunophenotypes. Remember the "owl's eye" appearance and the unique CD30+/CD15+/CD45- profile.

<a id='question-005'></a>

### Question 005: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-05 (❏Describe lymphoid, mature B cell, peripheral T ce)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of mature B-cell neoplasms based on morphology, immunophenotype, and genetic features, specifically distinguishing between Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (CLL/SLL) and other mature B-cell lymphomas. (识别成熟B细胞肿瘤，基于形态学、免疫表型和遗传学特征，特别是区分慢性淋巴细胞白血病/小淋巴细胞淋巴瘤 (CLL/SLL) 和其他成熟B细胞淋巴瘤。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 68-year-old male presents to his primary care physician for a routine check-up. He reports feeling unusually fatigued over the past few months and has noticed some painless swelling in his neck and armpits. His past medical history is unremarkable, and he denies any fever, night sweats, or weight loss. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and axillary regions. The spleen is palpable 3 cm below the left costal margin. Vital signs are stable. Complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell (WBC) count of 35,000/µL (normal range: 4,000-11,000/µL) with a differential showing 90% lymphocytes. Peripheral blood smear reveals numerous small, mature-appearing lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and scant cytoplasm. Flow cytometry on peripheral blood lymphocytes shows positivity for CD5, CD19, CD20 (dim), and CD23, and negativity for CD10, FMC7, and surface immunoglobulin (sIg). Cytogenetic analysis reveals a deletion in the short arm of chromosome 13 (del(13q)). Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Follicular Lymphoma
(B) Mantle Cell Lymphoma
(C) Burkitt Lymphoma
(D) Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (CLL/SLL)
(E) Hairy Cell Leukemia

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 68-year-old male. CLL/SLL is most common in older adults (median age ~70).
- **Symptoms:** Fatigue, painless lymphadenopathy (cervical, axillary), splenomegaly. These are classic B symptoms (though fever, night sweats, weight loss are absent here) and signs of lymphoproliferation common in CLL/SLL.
- **CBC:** High WBC count (35,000/µL) with marked lymphocytosis (90%). This indicates a clonal proliferation of lymphocytes.
- **Peripheral Smear:** Small, mature-appearing lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and scant cytoplasm. This morphology is characteristic of CLL/SLL cells (often described as "smudge cells" on smear due to fragility).
- **Flow Cytometry:** CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+, CD10-, FMC7-, sIg-. This immunophenotype is highly specific for CLL/SLL. CD5 is typically expressed on T cells but aberrantly on CLL B cells. CD23 is also characteristically positive. CD10 and FMC7 are typically negative, helping distinguish it from follicular lymphoma and mantle cell lymphoma, respectively. Dim CD20 expression is also typical.
- **Cytogenetics:** del(13q). This is the most common cytogenetic abnormality in CLL/SLL and is associated with a relatively favorable prognosis.
- **Conclusion:** The combination of age, clinical presentation (lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly, fatigue), marked lymphocytosis with characteristic morphology, specific immunophenotype (CD5+, CD23+), and del(13q) strongly points to CLL/SLL.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Pathophysiology:** CLL/SLL is a clonal neoplasm of mature B lymphocytes. These cells accumulate in the blood, bone marrow, lymph nodes, and spleen. The cells are typically resistant to apoptosis and exhibit proliferation signals.
- **Molecular Basis:** The pathogenesis involves genetic abnormalities that disrupt apoptosis regulation and lymphocyte signaling. Common genetic alterations include deletions (del(13q), del(11q), del(17p)), trisomy 12, and point mutations (e.g., in *IGHV*, *TP53*, *SF3B1*, *NOTCH1*). del(13q) involves loss of microRNAs (like miR-15a and miR-16-1) that target BCL2, an anti-apoptotic protein. Loss of these microRNAs leads to increased BCL2 expression, promoting cell survival.
- **Immunophenotype:** The characteristic immunophenotype (CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+) is crucial for diagnosis. CD5 expression is usually restricted to T cells and a subset of B-1 cells, but it is aberrantly expressed in CLL B cells. CD23 is a low-affinity IgE receptor, also typically positive in CLL.
- **Clinical Course:** CLL often has an indolent course, with many patients diagnosed incidentally. It can transform into a more aggressive lymphoma (Richter's transformation), often involving TP53 mutations.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Follicular Lymphoma:** This is a common indolent B-cell lymphoma typically presenting with generalized lymphadenopathy. However, it usually involves nodal architecture (follicular pattern), expresses CD10 and BCL6, and lacks CD5 and CD23 expression. The immunophenotype (CD10+, CD5-, CD23-) and morphology (centrocytes/centroblasts) are distinct from the case.
- (B) **Mantle Cell Lymphoma:** This is an aggressive B-cell lymphoma. It typically expresses CD5, but it is characteristically positive for cyclin D1 (due to *CCND1* translocation t(11;14)) and negative for CD23. The morphology often shows small to medium-sized lymphocytes with irregular nuclei. The immunophenotype (CD5+, CD23-) and presence of cyclin D1 positivity distinguish it from CLL/SLL.
- (C) **Burkitt Lymphoma:** This is a highly aggressive B-cell lymphoma characterized by a "starry sky" appearance on histology due to numerous macrophages engulfing apoptotic tumor cells. It typically expresses CD10, BCL6, and surface immunoglobulin (sIg), but is negative for CD5 and CD23. It is associated with *MYC* translocations (e.g., t(8;14)). The morphology, immunophenotype, and clinical aggressiveness are very different.
- (E) **Hairy Cell Leukemia:** This is a rare, indolent B-cell leukemia characterized by pancytopenia (often), splenomegaly, and distinctive "hairy" cells on peripheral smear (irregular cytoplasmic projections). The immunophenotype is typically CD5-, CD19+, CD20+, CD103+, CD11c+, CD25+. The morphology and immunophenotype are distinct from CLL/SLL.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | CLL/SLL                     | Follicular Lymphoma         | Mantle Cell Lymphoma        | Burkitt Lymphoma            | Hairy Cell Leukemia         |
|----------------------|-----------------------------|-----------------------------|-----------------------------|-----------------------------|-----------------------------|
| **Age**              | Older adults (median ~70)   | Middle-aged/Older           | Middle-aged/Older           | Children/Young Adults       | Middle-aged/Older           |
| **Presentation**     | Lymphocytosis, LAD, Splenomegaly | LAD, often incidental      | LAD, Splenomegaly           | Rapidly growing mass        | Pancytopenia, Splenomegaly |
| **Morphology**       | Small, mature lymphocytes   | Centrocytes/Centroblasts    | Small/Medium, irregular nuclei | Lymphoblasts, "starry sky" | "Hairy" cells               |
| **CD5**              | +                           | -                           | +                           | -                           | -                           |
| **CD10**             | -                           | +                           | -                           | +                           | -                           |
| **CD23**             | +                           | -                           | -                           | -                           | -                           |
| **Cyclin D1**        | -                           | -                           | +                           | -                           | -                           |
| **sIg**              | Dim/Negative                | +                           | +                           | +                           | +                           |
| **Key Genetics**     | del(13q), del(11q), del(17p) | t(14;18)                    | t(11;14)                    | t(8;14), t(2;8), t(8;22)    | BRAF V600E                 |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CLL/SLL is the most common leukemia in adults in Western countries. Diagnosis relies on recognizing the characteristic clinical presentation, peripheral blood morphology, and, crucially, the specific immunophenotype (CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+). Differentiating CLL/SLL from other mature B-cell lymphomas like follicular lymphoma (CD10+, CD23-), mantle cell lymphoma (CD5+, CD23-), and Burkitt lymphoma (CD10+, CD5-) requires careful attention to the immunophenotype and cytogenetic findings.

<a id='question-006'></a>

### Question 006: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-06 (cell neoplasms)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the specific cytogenetic abnormality (t(14;18)) and its molecular consequence (IgH-BCL2 translocation) leading to constitutive anti-apoptotic signaling in Follicular Lymphoma, differentiating it from other B-cell lymphomas with different genetic features and clinical presentations. (理解特异性细胞遗传学异常 (t(14;18)) 及其分子后果 (IgH-BCL2易位) 在滤泡性淋巴瘤中导致持续抗凋亡信号，并将其与其他具有不同遗传特征和临床表现的 B 细胞淋巴瘤区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 58-year-old woman presents to her primary care physician with painless swelling in her neck and axillae, which she noticed several months ago and has been gradually enlarging. She denies fever, night sweats, or weight loss (B symptoms). Physical examination reveals multiple, firm, non-tender lymph nodes in the cervical and axillary regions, ranging from 1 to 4 cm in diameter. Her spleen is palpable 2 cm below the left costal margin. Complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell count of 8,500/µL (normal range: 4,000-11,000/µL) with a normal differential. Serum lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) is mildly elevated at 280 U/L (normal range: 120-250 U/L). A biopsy of an enlarged cervical lymph node is performed. Histopathology shows effacement of the normal lymph node architecture by a proliferation of small to medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, vesicular chromatin, and inconspicuous nucleoli. The cells are arranged in a follicular pattern, with prominent vascular proliferation within the follicles. Immunohistochemistry reveals the neoplastic cells are positive for CD10, CD19, CD20, and BCL2, and negative for CD5 and BCL6. Cytogenetic analysis of the tumor cells reveals a translocation involving chromosomes 14 and 18, t(14;18)(q32;q21).

Which of the following molecular events is the direct consequence of the cytogenetic abnormality described and is responsible for the pathogenesis of this lymphoma?

(A) Overexpression of the MYC oncogene due to translocation with the IgH locus.
(B) Constitutive activation of the B-cell receptor signaling pathway due to mutations in BTK.
(C) Inhibition of apoptosis due to overexpression of the BCL2 protein.
(D) Dysregulation of cell cycle control due to mutations in the TP53 tumor suppressor gene.
(E) Increased proliferation due to overexpression of the cyclin D1 protein.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 58-year-old woman with painless, gradually enlarging lymphadenopathy (neck, axillae) and splenomegaly, without B symptoms. This presentation is typical for indolent (slow-growing) B-cell lymphomas.
- **Lab Findings:** Normal WBC count, mildly elevated LDH. Normal WBC count is common in follicular lymphoma, unlike some other lymphomas (e.g., CLL, hairy cell leukemia). Mildly elevated LDH can be seen but is often normal or only slightly elevated in indolent lymphomas.
- **Histopathology:** Effacement of lymph node architecture by small to medium-sized lymphoid cells in a follicular pattern with prominent vascular proliferation. This follicular pattern is the hallmark of follicular lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** Positive for CD10, CD19, CD20 (B-cell markers), and BCL2. Negative for CD5 (rules out CLL/SLL) and BCL6 (typically positive in germinal center B-cells, but negative in follicular lymphoma neoplastic cells). The strong positivity for BCL2 is a key feature.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(14;18)(q32;q21). This specific translocation is pathognomonic for follicular lymphoma.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular consequence of the t(14;18) translocation responsible for pathogenesis.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The translocation t(14;18)(q32;q21) juxtaposes the BCL2 gene (located on chromosome 18) with the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) locus (located on chromosome 14).
- The IgH locus is highly active in B-cells undergoing immunoglobulin gene rearrangement.
- This juxtaposition leads to the overexpression of the BCL2 protein due to its transcription being driven by the strong IgH enhancer elements.
- BCL2 is an anti-apoptotic protein. It inhibits apoptosis by binding to and sequestering pro-apoptotic proteins like BAX and BAK, preventing mitochondrial outer membrane permeabilization and caspase activation.
- Overexpression of BCL2 leads to increased survival of B-cells that would normally undergo apoptosis, contributing to the accumulation of neoplastic follicular lymphocytes and the development of follicular lymphoma.
- Therefore, the direct molecular consequence of t(14;18) is the overexpression of BCL2, leading to inhibition of apoptosis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Overexpression of the MYC oncogene due to translocation with the IgH locus. This describes the t(8;14) translocation found in Burkitt lymphoma, not follicular lymphoma. Burkitt lymphoma typically presents as a rapidly growing mass, often in the jaw or abdomen, and is characterized by a "starry sky" appearance on histology.
- (B) Constitutive activation of the B-cell receptor signaling pathway due to mutations in BTK. Mutations in BTK are characteristic of X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA), a primary immunodeficiency, and are also found in some cases of mantle cell lymphoma (often associated with t(11;14) involving CCND1). This is not the mechanism in follicular lymphoma.
- (D) Dysregulation of cell cycle control due to mutations in the TP53 tumor suppressor gene. TP53 mutations are common in many cancers, including some hematologic malignancies like acute myeloid leukemia (AML) and diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL), especially those arising from germinal center B-cells. However, TP53 mutations are not the primary driver event in follicular lymphoma; the t(14;18) translocation is.
- (E) Increased proliferation due to overexpression of the cyclin D1 protein. Overexpression of cyclin D1 is typically caused by the t(11;14) translocation involving the CCND1 gene (encoding cyclin D1) and the IgH locus. This is characteristic of mantle cell lymphoma, which typically presents with diffuse lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly, and often involves the bone marrow and peripheral blood (leukemic phase). Mantle cell lymphoma cells are typically CD5 positive, unlike follicular lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Follicular Lymphoma (FL) | Burkitt Lymphoma (BL) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (MCL) | Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL) |
|----------------------|--------------------------|------------------------|-----------------------------|---------------------------------------|
| **Cytogenetic**      | t(14;18)                 | t(8;14)                | t(11;14)                    | Variable (often none specific)        |
| **Gene Involved**    | BCL2                     | MYC                   | CCND1 (Cyclin D1)           | Variable (e.g., MYC, BCL2, BCL6)      |
| **Molecular Event**  | IgH-BCL2 fusion          | IgH-MYC fusion         | IgH-CCND1 fusion            | Variable                             |
| **Pathogenesis**     | Anti-apoptosis           | Uncontrolled proliferation | Cell cycle dysregulation    | Variable                             |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD10+, BCL2+, BCL6-       | CD10-, BCL2-, BCL6-     | CD5+, Cyclin D1+            | Variable (often CD10+, BCL6+)         |
| **Histology**        | Follicular pattern        | Starry sky             | Mantle zone effacement      | Diffuse sheets                        |
| **Clinical Course**  | Indolent                 | Aggressive             | Intermediate/Aggressive     | Aggressive                            |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Follicular lymphoma is characterized by the t(14;18) translocation, leading to BCL2 overexpression and inhibition of apoptosis. This is a key high-yield association for USMLE Step 1. Remember to differentiate it from Burkitt lymphoma (t(8;14), MYC overexpression) and Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(11;14), Cyclin D1 overexpression).

---

<a id='question-007'></a>

### Question 007: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Hodgkin Lymphoma
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of Reed-Sternberg cells and their characteristic immunophenotype in the context of Hodgkin lymphoma, differentiating it from other lymphomas and reactive lymphoid proliferations. (识别里德-施坦贝格细胞及其特征性免疫表型，区分霍奇金淋巴瘤与其他淋巴瘤和反应性淋巴组织增生。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old male presents to his primary care physician with a painless, enlarging mass in his left supraclavicular region, noted over the past 3 months. He also reports intermittent, drenching night sweats and unintentional weight loss of approximately 10% of his body weight over the same period. He denies fever, cough, or shortness of breath. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender lymph node mass measuring 4 cm in diameter in the left supraclavicular fossa. The remainder of the physical exam is unremarkable. Complete blood count (CBC) shows mild normocytic anemia (Hgb 11.5 g/dL, normal range 13.5-17.5 g/dL) and a normal white blood cell count (WBC 6,500/µL, normal range 4,000-11,000/µL) with a normal differential. A CT scan of the chest, abdomen, and pelvis reveals enlarged mediastinal and hilar lymph nodes. An excisional biopsy of the supraclavicular lymph node is performed. Histopathological examination shows a cellular infiltrate composed predominantly of non-neoplastic lymphocytes, eosinophils, plasma cells, and histiocytes, interspersed with occasional large, atypical cells with abundant eosinophilic cytoplasm, prominent nucleoli, and bilobed or multilobed nuclei. Immunohistochemical staining of the atypical cells reveals positivity for CD30 and CD15, and negativity for CD45 (LCA) and most B-cell and T-cell markers.

Which of the following cellular features is most characteristic of the atypical cells described in this patient's lymph node biopsy?

(A) Expression of PAX5 and CD20, consistent with a B-cell origin.
(B) Expression of CD3 and CD7, consistent with a T-cell origin.
(C) Expression of CD30 and CD15, along with negativity for CD45.
(D) Expression of CD10 and BCL6, characteristic of follicular lymphoma.
(E) Expression of CD25 and IL-2 receptor alpha chain, characteristic of adult T-cell leukemia/lymphoma.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 35-year-old male. Age is typical for classical Hodgkin lymphoma (bimodal distribution: young adulthood and older age).
- **Symptoms:** Painless lymphadenopathy (supraclavicular), night sweats, weight loss ("B symptoms"). These are classic symptoms associated with Hodgkin lymphoma.
- **Physical Exam:** Firm, non-tender lymph node mass. Consistent with lymphoma.
- **Labs:** Mild anemia, normal WBC count. Can be seen in Hodgkin lymphoma, but not specific.
- **Imaging:** Enlarged mediastinal and hilar lymph nodes. Common finding in Hodgkin lymphoma.
- **Biopsy:** Cellular infiltrate with non-neoplastic background cells (lymphocytes, eosinophils, plasma cells, histiocytes) and occasional large, atypical cells. This description is highly suggestive of Hodgkin lymphoma.
- **Atypical Cell Morphology:** Abundant eosinophilic cytoplasm, prominent nucleoli, bilobed or multilobed nuclei. This describes Reed-Sternberg (RS) cells, the hallmark cells of Hodgkin lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** Positivity for CD30 and CD15, negativity for CD45 (LCA) and most B/T cell markers. This immunophenotype is characteristic of classical Hodgkin lymphoma RS cells.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the most characteristic cellular feature (immunophenotype) of the atypical cells (RS cells).

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Hodgkin Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Hodgkin lymphoma is characterized by the presence of malignant Reed-Sternberg (RS) cells within a reactive inflammatory background. RS cells are derived from germinal center B cells, although they typically lose expression of pan-B-cell markers like CD20 and CD45 (LCA) in classical Hodgkin lymphoma.
- **RS Cell Immunophenotype:** Classical RS cells characteristically express CD30 and CD15. CD30 is a member of the TNF receptor superfamily, and CD15 (Lewis antigen) is a carbohydrate antigen. They are negative for CD45 (leukocyte common antigen), which is expressed on most hematopoietic cells. They also typically lack expression of B-cell markers like CD20 and T-cell markers like CD3.
- **Differential Diagnosis:** The key is differentiating RS cells from other malignant lymphoid cells.
    - **B-cell lymphomas (e.g., Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma, Follicular Lymphoma):** These cells express B-cell markers like CD20 and CD79a, and typically CD45.
    - **T-cell lymphomas (e.g., Peripheral T-cell Lymphoma, Adult T-cell Leukemia/Lymphoma):** These cells express T-cell markers like CD3 and CD7, and typically CD45.
    - **Reactive Lymphoid Hyperplasia:** Contains reactive lymphocytes, histiocytes, etc., but lacks the characteristic RS cells and their specific immunophenotype.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Expression of PAX5 and CD20, consistent with a B-cell origin. Incorrect. While RS cells are thought to originate from B cells, classical RS cells typically *lose* expression of pan-B-cell markers like CD20 and PAX5. CD20 positivity would suggest a B-cell lymphoma, not classical Hodgkin lymphoma.
- (B): Expression of CD3 and CD7, consistent with a T-cell origin. Incorrect. CD3 and CD7 are T-cell markers. RS cells are not of T-cell origin and do not express these markers.
- (D): Expression of CD10 and BCL6, characteristic of follicular lymphoma. Incorrect. CD10 and BCL6 are markers typically expressed in germinal center B-cell lymphomas, such as follicular lymphoma. RS cells do not express this combination of markers.
- (E): Expression of CD25 and IL-2 receptor alpha chain, characteristic of adult T-cell leukemia/lymphoma. Incorrect. CD25 (IL-2Rα) is strongly expressed in Adult T-cell Leukemia/Lymphoma (ATLL), a T-cell malignancy associated with HTLV-1. RS cells do not express CD25.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Classical Hodgkin Lymphoma (RS Cells) | Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma | Follicular Lymphoma | Adult T-cell Leukemia/Lymphoma |
|----------------------|---------------------------------------|---------------------------------|----------------------|---------------------------------|
| **Hallmark Cell**   | Reed-Sternberg Cell                   | Large atypical B-cell           | Centrocytes/Centroblasts | Mature T-cells                  |
| **Background**      | Reactive (lymphocytes, eosinophils)   | Diffuse infiltrate              | Follicular pattern   | Infiltrate, often pleomorphic T-cells |
| **CD30**             | Positive (+)                          | Variable (+/-)                  | Negative (-)         | Negative (-)                    |
| **CD15**             | Positive (+)                          | Negative (-)                    | Negative (-)         | Negative (-)                    |
| **CD45 (LCA)**       | Negative (-)                          | Positive (+)                    | Positive (+)         | Positive (+)                    |
| **CD20**             | Negative (-)                          | Positive (+)                    | Positive (+)         | Negative (-)                    |
| **CD3/CD7**          | Negative (-)                          | Negative (-)                    | Negative (-)         | Positive (+)                    |
| **PAX5**             | Negative (-)                          | Positive (+)                    | Positive (+)         | Negative (-)                    |
| **CD10/BCL6**        | Negative (-)                          | Variable (+/-)                  | Positive (+)         | Negative (-)                    |
| **CD25 (IL-2Rα)**    | Negative (-)                          | Negative (-)                    | Negative (-)         | Positive (+)                    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Hodgkin lymphoma is defined by the presence of Reed-Sternberg cells, which have a characteristic immunophenotype: CD30+, CD15+, CD45-, and typically CD20-. This distinguishes them from other lymphomas like B-cell lymphomas (CD20+) and T-cell lymphomas (CD3+). Key differentials include other lymphomas presenting with lymphadenopathy (e.g., DLBCL, Follicular Lymphoma, ATLL) and reactive lymphoid hyperplasia. Always consider the specific immunophenotype when evaluating lymphoid neoplasms.

<a id='question-008'></a>

### Question 008: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-08 (B and T lymphoblasticlymphoma/leukemia,and myeloid)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating between acute myeloid leukemia (AML) subtypes based on cytogenetic abnormalities and their prognostic significance, specifically focusing on t(8;21) and inv(16) as favorable risk factors. (区分AML亚型，基于细胞遗传学异常及其预后意义，特别是t(8;21)和inv(16)作为有利预后因素。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to the clinic complaining of fatigue, easy bruising, and recurrent infections over the past month. His past medical history is unremarkable, and he denies any significant family history of hematologic malignancies. Physical examination reveals pallor, petechiae on the lower extremities, and mild splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows: WBC 45,000/µL (normal range: 4,000-11,000/µL) with a left shift, hemoglobin 8.5 g/dL (normal range: 13.5-17.5 g/dL), and platelets 25,000/µL (normal range: 150,000-450,000/µL). A peripheral blood smear shows numerous blasts with Auer rods. Bone marrow biopsy confirms acute myeloid leukemia (AML). Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the presence of a translocation between chromosomes 8 and 21, t(8;21)(q22;q22.1). Which of the following statements regarding this patient's AML is most likely correct?

(A) This translocation is associated with a poor prognosis and is commonly found in AML with myelomonocytic differentiation (M4).
(B) This translocation results in the fusion of the RUNX1T1 gene on chromosome 8 with the PML gene on chromosome 15, leading to abnormal myeloid differentiation.
(C) This translocation is associated with a favorable prognosis and is commonly found in AML with eosinophilic differentiation (M2).
(D) This translocation is associated with a poor prognosis and is commonly found in AML with megakaryoblastic differentiation (M7).
(E) This translocation is associated with a favorable prognosis and is commonly found in AML with monocytic differentiation (M5).

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with fatigue, bruising, infections (suggestive of pancytopenia and immune dysfunction).
- **Physical Exam:** Pallor (anemia), petechiae (thrombocytopenia), splenomegaly (possible extramedullary hematopoiesis or leukemic infiltration).
- **CBC:** Leukocytosis with left shift (immature cells), anemia, thrombocytopenia – classic findings for acute leukemia.
- **Peripheral Smear:** Blasts with Auer rods – characteristic of AML.
- **Bone Marrow Biopsy:** Confirms AML.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(8;21)(q22;q22.1) – This is the crucial finding. The question asks about the implications of this specific translocation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **t(8;21)(q22;q22.1):** This translocation involves the fusion of the *RUNX1T1* gene (formerly *AML1*, located on chromosome 8q22) and the *ETV6* gene (formerly *TEL*, located on chromosome 21q22.1).
- **RUNX1T1:** Encodes a transcription factor crucial for normal hematopoiesis and myeloid differentiation.
- **ETV6:** Encodes a transcription factor involved in various developmental processes.
- **Fusion Protein:** The resulting RUNX1T1-ETV6 fusion protein disrupts normal hematopoietic regulation, leading to the proliferation of myeloid blasts and the development of AML.
- **Prognosis:** AML with t(8;21) is generally associated with a *favorable* prognosis, especially in younger patients, and often responds well to standard chemotherapy.
- **WHO Classification:** This translocation is most commonly associated with AML with maturation (formerly M2 subtype), characterized by eosinophilic precursors in the bone marrow.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. t(8;21) is associated with a *favorable* prognosis, not poor. It is most commonly found in AML with maturation (M2), not typically M4 (myelomonocytic).
- (B): Incorrect. The translocation involves *RUNX1T1* (chromosome 8) and *ETV6* (chromosome 21), not *PML* (chromosome 15). The t(15;17) translocation involves *PML* and *RARA* and is characteristic of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL).
- (C): Correct. t(8;21) is indeed associated with a favorable prognosis and is classically linked to AML with maturation (M2), often showing eosinophilic features.
- (D): Incorrect. t(8;21) is associated with a *favorable* prognosis, not poor. It is not typically found in AML with megakaryoblastic differentiation (M7). M7 is often associated with abnormalities of chromosome 3 or 3q abnormalities.
- (E): Incorrect. While t(8;21) is associated with a favorable prognosis, it is most commonly found in AML with maturation (M2), not typically M5 (monocytic). M5 is often associated with abnormalities of chromosome 11q23 (MLL gene rearrangements).

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | t(8;21) AML (M2) | t(15;17) AML (APL/M3) | inv(16) AML (M2) | t(11q23) AML (M5) |
|----------------------|-------------------|------------------------|-------------------|-------------------|
| **Translocation**    | t(8;21)           | t(15;17)               | inv(16) or t(16;16)| t(11q23)          |
| **Gene Fusion**      | RUNX1T1-ETV6      | PML-RARA               | CBFB-MYH11        | KMT2A (MLL) rearrangements |
| **Prognosis**        | Favorable         | Favorable (with ATRA)  | Favorable         | Poor              |
| **WHO Subtype**      | AML w/ maturation | APL                    | AML w/ maturation | AML w/ monocytic differentiation |
| **Characteristic Feature** | Eosinophils      | Auer rods, promyelocytes | Eosinophils      | Monoblasts/promonocytes |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The translocation t(8;21)(q22;q22.1) in AML, resulting in the RUNX1T1-ETV6 fusion protein, is a key cytogenetic abnormality associated with a favorable prognosis and is typically found in AML with maturation (M2 subtype). Differentiating AML subtypes based on cytogenetics is crucial for risk stratification and treatment planning. Key differentials include t(15;17) (APL, M3), inv(16) (M2), and t(11q23) (M5), each with distinct genetic features and prognostic implications.

<a id='question-009'></a>

### Question 009: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-09 (❏Solve problems concerning diseases of histiocytes)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the pathogenesis, morphology, and immunohistochemical profile of Langerhans cell histiocytosis (LCH), differentiating it from other histiocytic disorders and malignancies. (理解朗格汉斯细胞组织细胞增生症 (LCH) 的发病机制、形态学和免疫组织化学特征，并将其与其他组织细胞疾病和恶性肿瘤区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents to the pediatrician with a 3-month history of persistent, non-productive cough and mild shortness of breath. His parents also report episodes of low-grade fever and night sweats. Physical examination reveals hepatosplenomegaly and multiple small, erythematous, scaly papules on his scalp and trunk. A chest radiograph shows diffuse interstitial infiltrates and hilar lymphadenopathy. A bone marrow biopsy is performed. Microscopic examination reveals numerous neoplastic cells with abundant eosinophilic cytoplasm, vesicular nuclei with prominent nucleoli, and characteristic grooved chromatin. Immunohistochemical staining shows these cells are positive for CD1a, S100, and Langerin (CD207), but negative for CD45, CD3, CD20, CD30, and CD68. Which of the following is the most likely underlying pathogenic mechanism driving this condition?

(A) Somatic mutation in the *BRAF* gene leading to constitutive activation of the MAPK pathway.
(B) Fusion of the *BCR* and *ABL1* genes resulting in a constitutively active tyrosine kinase.
(C) Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection leading to immortalization of B lymphocytes.
(D) Aberrant expression of CD30 on Reed-Sternberg cells, driven by a translocation involving the *JAKB* gene.
(E) Defective phagocytosis and lysosomal function in macrophages due to mutations in the *NPC1* gene.

#### Correct Answer: A

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 3-year-old boy - LCH typically affects children and young adults.
- **Symptoms:** Persistent cough, shortness of breath, low-grade fever, night sweats - Suggestive of pulmonary involvement and systemic symptoms common in LCH.
- **Physical Exam:** Hepatosplenomegaly, scaly papules on scalp/trunk - Classic skin lesions (seborrheic dermatitis-like) and organomegaly are common findings in LCH.
- **Chest Radiograph:** Diffuse interstitial infiltrates, hilar lymphadenopathy - Consistent with pulmonary LCH, often presenting as interstitial lung disease.
- **Bone Marrow Biopsy:**
    - **Morphology:** Neoplastic cells with abundant eosinophilic cytoplasm, vesicular nuclei, prominent nucleoli, grooved chromatin - Characteristic morphology of Langerhans cells.
    - **Immunohistochemistry:** Positive for CD1a, S100, Langerin (CD207) - These are specific markers for Langerhans cells and are crucial for diagnosing LCH. Negative for CD45 (leukocyte common antigen), CD3, CD20 (B cells), CD30 (Hodgkin lymphoma), CD68 (macrophages) - This helps rule out other hematopoietic malignancies and reactive processes.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the most likely underlying pathogenic mechanism.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Langerhans Cell Histiocytosis (LCH):** A clonal proliferation of Langerhans cells, which are specialized dendritic cells found in the epidermis and other tissues.
- **Pathogenesis:** LCH is considered a neoplastic disorder. The underlying mechanism involves a somatic mutation in the *BRAF* gene, specifically the *BRAFV600E* mutation, which occurs in approximately 50-60% of cases. This mutation leads to constitutive activation of the mitogen-activated protein kinase (MAPK) pathway, promoting uncontrolled proliferation and survival of Langerhans cells. The MAPK pathway is crucial for cell growth, differentiation, and survival.
- **Clinical Features:** Can range from solitary bone lesions to multisystem disease involving skin, bone, lung, liver, spleen, lymph nodes, and CNS. The presentation in the vignette (pulmonary, skin, systemic symptoms, hepatosplenomegaly) is typical for multisystem LCH.
- **Diagnosis:** Based on clinical presentation, imaging, and histopathology. The characteristic morphology and immunohistochemical profile (CD1a+, S100+, Langerin+) are diagnostic.
- **Treatment:** Varies depending on the extent of disease. Options include observation, corticosteroids, chemotherapy (e.g., vinblastine, etoposide), targeted therapy (e.g., vemurafenib for *BRAF* V600E mutated cases), and surgery.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Somatic mutation in the *BRAF* gene leading to constitutive activation of the MAPK pathway.** This is the correct answer. The *BRAFV600E* mutation is a well-established driver mutation in a significant proportion of LCH cases, leading to MAPK pathway activation, which is central to the pathogenesis.
- (B) **Fusion of the *BCR* and *ABL1* genes resulting in a constitutively active tyrosine kinase.** This describes the Philadelphia chromosome and the underlying mechanism of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML). CML involves myeloid progenitor cells, not Langerhans cells, and has a different morphology and immunophenotype (CD45+, CD34+, myeloperoxidase+).
- (C) **Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection leading to immortalization of B lymphocytes.** EBV is associated with several lymphomas, including Burkitt lymphoma, Hodgkin lymphoma, and post-transplant lymphoproliferative disorder (PTLD), all involving B lymphocytes. LCH involves Langerhans cells (dendritic cells), not B lymphocytes, and is not primarily driven by EBV (though EBV can sometimes be detected in LCH lesions, its role is not considered primary).
- (D) **Aberrant expression of CD30 on Reed-Sternberg cells, driven by a translocation involving the *JAKB* gene.** CD30 is a marker often expressed on Reed-Sternberg cells in Hodgkin lymphoma. While *JAKB* mutations can occur in certain lymphomas, they are not the primary driver in classical Hodgkin lymphoma, and the cells involved (Reed-Sternberg cells) are derived from B lymphocytes, not Langerhans cells. The morphology and immunophenotype described in the vignette are inconsistent with Hodgkin lymphoma.
- (E) **Defective phagocytosis and lysosomal function in macrophages due to mutations in the *NPC1* gene.** This describes Niemann-Pick disease type C, a lysosomal storage disorder affecting macrophages. While histiocytes (macrophages) are involved, the clinical presentation, morphology (lipid-laden macrophages), and immunophenotype (CD68+) are distinct from LCH. LCH involves Langerhans cells, not primarily macrophages with defective phagocytosis.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | Langerhans Cell Histiocytosis (LCH) | Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) | EBV-Associated Lymphoma | Hodgkin Lymphoma | Niemann-Pick Disease Type C |
|----------------------|--------------------------------------|---------------------------------|--------------------------|-------------------|-----------------------------|
| **Cell Type**        | Langerhans Cells (Dendritic Cells)   | Myeloid Progenitors             | B Lymphocytes            | Reed-Sternberg Cells (B cell origin) | Macrophages                  |
| **Key Markers**      | CD1a+, S100+, Langerin (CD207)+      | CD45+, CD34+, MPO+              | CD19+, CD20+ (usually)   | CD30+, CD15+      | CD68+                       |
| **Genetic Driver**   | *BRAF* V600E mutation (common)       | *BCR-ABL1* fusion               | EBV infection            | Variable (e.g., *JAKB* mutations in some) | *NPC1* mutation              |
| **Pathophysiology**  | MAPK pathway activation             | Constitutive tyrosine kinase    | Viral immortalization    | Aberrant CD30 expression | Lysosomal storage            |
| **Typical Age**      | Children/Young Adults                | Adults                          | Variable                 | Bimodal (young adult, elderly) | Childhood/Adulthood          |
| **Clinical Features**| Skin lesions, bone pain, lung disease | Splenomegaly, fatigue, leukocytosis | Lymphadenopathy, systemic symptoms | Lymphadenopathy, B symptoms | Hepatosplenomegaly, neurological symptoms |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
LCH is a clonal proliferation of Langerhans cells characterized by specific morphology and immunophenotype (CD1a+, S100+, Langerin+). The *BRAF* V600E mutation is a key pathogenic driver in many cases. Differentiating LCH from other histiocytic disorders (like Niemann-Pick C) and hematopoietic malignancies (like CML, lymphomas) relies on recognizing the specific cell type, morphology, and immunophenotype.
