<a id='question-038'></a>

### Question 038: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-38 (Behavioral Science/Social Sciences)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular pathogenesis of specific leukemias, particularly the role of chromosomal translocations and their resulting fusion proteins in driving leukemogenesis. (理解特定白血病的分子发病机制，特别是染色体易位及其产生的融合蛋白在驱动白血病发生发展中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old female presents to her primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and recurrent infections over the past 3 months. She denies fever, weight loss, or bone pain. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and axillary regions, and mild splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows leukocytosis (WBC 85,000/µL, normal range 4,000-11,000/µL) with a predominance of immature lymphocytes (blasts 60%). Peripheral blood smear reveals cells with fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm, some with Auer rods. Cytogenetic analysis of bone marrow aspirate shows the t(15;17) translocation.

Which of the following molecular events is most directly responsible for the pathogenesis of this patient's condition?

(A) Formation of the BCR-ABL fusion protein, leading to constitutive tyrosine kinase activity.
(B) Formation of the PML-RARα fusion protein, leading to aberrant retinoic acid signaling.
(C) Formation of the RUNX1-RUNX1T1 fusion protein, leading to impaired hematopoietic differentiation.
(D) Formation of the ETV6-RUNX1 fusion protein, leading to dysregulation of transcription factors.
(E) Formation of the MYC-IGH fusion protein, leading to overexpression of a proto-oncogene.

#### Correct Answer: (B)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 32-year-old female. Age is relevant as it fits the typical age range for Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL).
*   **Symptoms:** Fatigue, night sweats, recurrent infections. These are common constitutional symptoms associated with leukemia due to bone marrow failure and immune dysfunction.
*   **Physical Exam:** Generalized lymphadenopathy and mild splenomegaly. While lymphadenopathy is more typical of lymphomas, it can occur in leukemias, especially those with extramedullary involvement. Splenomegaly is common in many hematologic malignancies.
*   **CBC:** Leukocytosis (85,000/µL) with a high percentage of blasts (60%). This indicates an acute leukemia.
*   **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Immature lymphocytes (blasts) with fine chromatin, inconspicuous nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. This morphology is characteristic of myeloblasts. The presence of Auer rods is a key feature, strongly suggesting Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML).
*   **Cytogenetics:** t(15;17) translocation. This specific translocation is pathognomonic for Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of AML (specifically AML M3 according to the older FAB classification).
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular event responsible for the pathogenesis. Given the diagnosis of APL based on the t(15;17) translocation, the question targets the specific fusion protein created by this translocation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   The t(15;17) translocation involves the fusion of the *PML* gene on chromosome 15 with the *RARA* (retinoic acid receptor alpha) gene on chromosome 17.
*   This fusion creates the *PML-RARA* fusion gene, which encodes the PML-RARα fusion protein.
*   The PML-RARα fusion protein retains the DNA-binding domain of RARα but lacks the activation function domain 2 (AF-2).
*   This abnormal fusion protein binds to retinoic acid response elements (RAREs) on DNA, recruiting corepressor complexes (like NCoR and SMRT) instead of coactivators.
*   This leads to the repression of genes normally activated by retinoic acid, effectively blocking myeloid differentiation at the promyelocyte stage.
*   The accumulation of promyelocytes in the bone marrow and peripheral blood is the hallmark of APL.
*   Treatment with all-trans retinoic acid (ATRA) overcomes this repression by binding to the PML-RARα fusion protein, inducing a conformational change that allows it to recruit coactivators, thereby promoting myeloid differentiation and leading to remission.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) Formation of the BCR-ABL fusion protein, leading to constitutive tyrosine kinase activity.** This is the hallmark of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and Philadelphia chromosome-positive Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (Ph+ ALL), resulting from the t(9;22) translocation. The BCR-ABL fusion protein is a constitutively active tyrosine kinase that drives uncontrolled proliferation of myeloid cells. This is incorrect because the patient has t(15;17), not t(9;22).
*   **(C) Formation of the RUNX1-RUNX1T1 fusion protein, leading to impaired hematopoietic differentiation.** This fusion protein is characteristic of Acute Myeloid Leukemia with t(8;21), a common subtype of AML. RUNX1 and RUNX1T1 (ETO) are transcription factors involved in hematopoiesis. Their fusion disrupts normal differentiation. This is incorrect because the patient has t(15;17).
*   **(D) Formation of the ETV6-RUNX1 fusion protein, leading to dysregulation of transcription factors.** This fusion protein is characteristic of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia with t(12;21), the most common genetic abnormality in childhood ALL. ETV6 and RUNX1 are transcription factors. This is incorrect because the patient has t(15;17) and presents with features suggestive of AML (blasts with Auer rods).
*   **(E) Formation of the MYC-IGH fusion protein, leading to overexpression of a proto-oncogene.** This fusion occurs in Burkitt lymphoma, resulting from translocations involving the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8 and immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) locus on chromosome 14 (t(8;14)) or light chain loci (t(2;8) or t(8;22)). This leads to MYC overexpression, driving B-cell proliferation. This is incorrect because the patient has t(15;17) and presents with features suggestive of AML.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | APL (t(15;17))                  | CML (t(9;22))                  | AML t(8;21)                    | ALL t(12;21)                    | Burkitt Lymphoma (t(8;14))      |
| :------------------ | :------------------------------ | :------------------------------ | :------------------------------ | :------------------------------ | :------------------------------ |
| **Translocation**   | t(15;17)                        | t(9;22)                         | t(8;21)                         | t(12;21)                        | t(8;14) or t(2;8) or t(8;22)     |
| **Fusion Protein**  | PML-RARα                        | BCR-ABL                         | RUNX1-RUNX1T1                   | ETV6-RUNX1                      | MYC-IGH                         |
| **Pathophysiology** | Blocked myeloid differentiation | Constitutive tyrosine kinase    | Impaired differentiation        | Impaired differentiation        | MYC overexpression              |
| **Cell Type**       | Myeloid (Promyelocytes)          | Myeloid                         | Myeloid                         | Lymphoid                        | Lymphoid (B-cells)              |
| **Key Feature**     | Auer rods, ATRA sensitivity     | Philadelphia chromosome         | Eosinophilia often present      | Common in children             | Jaw mass, "starry sky" histology |
| **Clinical**        | DIC common                      | Chronic phase, blast crisis      | Variable                       | Variable                       | Rapidly growing tumor           |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The t(15;17) translocation in APL leads to the formation of the PML-RARα fusion protein, which blocks myeloid differentiation at the promyelocyte stage. This is a critical association for USMLE Step 1. Differentiating APL from other AML subtypes and CML based on cytogenetics and clinical features (like Auer rods and DIC risk in APL) is high-yield. Remember the specific fusion proteins associated with common translocations in leukemia and lymphoma.

<a id='question-039'></a>

### Question 039: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-39 (• Increased lymphocytes(lymphocytosis)occurs with )
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating chronic lymphoproliferative disorders based on morphology, immunophenotype, and cytogenetics, specifically distinguishing Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (CLL/SLL) from other lymphoproliferative disorders presenting with lymphocytosis. (• 慢性淋巴增生性疾病的鉴别诊断，基于形态学、免疫表型和细胞遗传学，特别是区分慢性淋巴细胞白血病/小淋巴细胞淋巴瘤 (CLL/SLL) 与其他表现出淋巴细胞增多的淋巴增生性疾病。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 68-year-old male presents to his primary care physician for a routine check-up. He reports feeling generally well but has noticed increased fatigue over the past few months. He denies fever, night sweats, weight loss, or recent infections. His past medical history is significant for hypertension, well-controlled with lisinopril. Physical examination reveals mild splenomegaly. Vital signs are within normal limits. A complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell (WBC) count of 28,000/µL (normal range: 4,000-11,000/µL) with a differential showing 90% lymphocytes. The absolute lymphocyte count is 25,200/µL. Red blood cell count and platelet count are within normal limits. Peripheral blood smear examination reveals numerous small, mature-appearing lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and scant cytoplasm. Flow cytometry on the peripheral blood lymphocytes shows positivity for CD5, CD19, CD20 (dim), and CD23, and negativity for CD10, FMC7, and surface immunoglobulin (sIg). Cytogenetic analysis reveals a deletion of 13q14 in 50% of cells. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Hairy Cell Leukemia
(B) Mantle Cell Lymphoma
(C) Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma
(D) Follicular Lymphoma
(E) Adult T-cell Leukemia/Lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 68-year-old male, typical age for CLL/SLL.
*   **Symptoms:** Fatigue, mild splenomegaly, no B symptoms (fever, night sweats, weight loss). This presentation is characteristic of indolent chronic lymphoproliferative disorders like CLL/SLL.
*   **CBC:** Marked lymphocytosis (WBC 28,000/µL, 90% lymphocytes, absolute count 25,200/µL). This points towards a lymphoproliferative disorder.
*   **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Small, mature-appearing lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and scant cytoplasm. This morphology is classic for CLL/SLL cells (often described as "smudge cells" on smear due to fragility).
*   **Flow Cytometry:** CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+, CD10-, FMC7-, sIg-. This immunophenotype is highly characteristic of CLL/SLL. CD5 is typically expressed on T cells but aberrantly on CLL B cells. CD23 is also typically positive in CLL. CD10 and FMC7 are usually negative, helping to distinguish it from other B-cell lymphomas like Follicular Lymphoma and Mantle Cell Lymphoma. Dim CD20 expression is also typical.
*   **Cytogenetics:** Deletion of 13q14 is a common cytogenetic abnormality found in CLL/SLL.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the most likely diagnosis based on the constellation of clinical, hematological, morphological, immunophenotypic, and cytogenetic findings.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **CLL/SLL Pathophysiology:** CLL/SLL is a clonal B-cell lymphoproliferative disorder characterized by the accumulation of mature-appearing but functionally incompetent B lymphocytes in the blood, bone marrow, and lymphoid tissues. The cells express CD5, CD19, CD20 (dim), and CD23. The disease is often indolent but can transform into a more aggressive lymphoma (Richter's transformation).
*   **Molecular Basis:** Common genetic abnormalities include deletions of 13q14 (RB1 gene), 11q22 (ATM gene), 17p (TP53 gene), and trisomy 12. These mutations contribute to cell cycle dysregulation, apoptosis evasion, and proliferation.
*   **Immunophenotype Significance:** The specific combination of CD markers (CD5+, CD19+, CD20dim+, CD23+) is the hallmark immunophenotype for CLL/SLL, distinguishing it from other B-cell lymphomas.
*   **Clinical Course:** Often asymptomatic at diagnosis, discovered incidentally on CBC. Progression is variable, with some patients remaining stable for years, while others progress rapidly.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) Hairy Cell Leukemia:** This is a B-cell leukemia characterized by pancytopenia (not lymphocytosis), splenomegaly, and distinctive "hairy" cells on peripheral smear (irregular cytoplasmic projections). Immunophenotype is typically CD19+, CD20+, CD22+, CD11c+, CD25+, CD103+, and negative for CD5 and CD23. This profile does not match the patient's findings.
*   **(B) Mantle Cell Lymphoma:** This is an aggressive B-cell lymphoma. While it can present with lymphocytosis, the typical immunophenotype is CD19+, CD20+, CD5+, FMC7+, CD10-, CD23-, and positive for cyclin D1 (due to t(11;14) translocation). The patient's immunophenotype (CD23+, FMC7-) is inconsistent with Mantle Cell Lymphoma.
*   **(D) Follicular Lymphoma:** This is an indolent B-cell lymphoma typically presenting with lymphadenopathy. It can involve the blood (leukemic phase), but the characteristic cells are centroblasts and centrocytes. The immunophenotype is typically CD19+, CD20+, CD10+, BCL2+, CD5-, CD23-. The patient's immunophenotype (CD5+, CD10-, CD23+) is inconsistent with Follicular Lymphoma.
*   **(E) Adult T-cell Leukemia/Lymphoma (ATLL):** This is a malignancy of mature CD4+ T lymphocytes caused by Human T-lymphotropic virus type 1 (HTLV-1). It typically presents with generalized lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly, skin lesions, and hypercalcemia. The cells are T lymphocytes (CD3+, CD4+, CD8-), not B lymphocytes. The patient's findings are characteristic of a B-cell disorder, not a T-cell disorder.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | CLL/SLL                      | Hairy Cell Leukemia        | Mantle Cell Lymphoma       | Follicular Lymphoma        | ATLL                          |
| :------------------ | :--------------------------- | :------------------------- | :------------------------- | :------------------------- | :---------------------------- |
| **Cell Type**       | Mature B-lymphocytes         | B-lymphocytes (hairy cells) | Mature B-lymphocytes       | Mature B-lymphocytes       | Mature CD4+ T-lymphocytes    |
| **Typical CBC**     | Lymphocytosis                | Pancytopenia               | Lymphocytosis (variable)   | Lymphocytosis (variable)   | Lymphocytosis (variable)      |
| **Morphology**      | Small, mature, clumped chromatin | Hairy projections          | Variable, often small/medium | Centroblasts/centrocytes   | Pleomorphic T-cells           |
| **CD5**             | +                            | -                         | +                         | -                         | -                             |
| **CD10**            | -                            | -                         | -                         | +                         | -                             |
| **CD23**            | +                            | -                         | -                         | -                         | -                             |
| **FMC7**            | -                            | -                         | +                         | -                         | -                             |
| **sIg**             | Dim/Negative                 | +                         | +                         | +                         | -                             |
| **Common Genetics** | del(13q), del(11q), del(17p) | BRAF V600E                 | t(11;14) (CCND1)           | t(14;18) (BCL2)            | HTLV-1 integration           |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The classic presentation of CLL/SLL involves an elderly patient with asymptomatic lymphocytosis, small mature lymphocytes on smear, and a characteristic immunophenotype (CD5+, CD19+, CD20dim+, CD23+). Differentiating CLL/SLL from other lymphoproliferative disorders requires careful attention to morphology, immunophenotype, and cytogenetics. Key differentials include Mantle Cell Lymphoma (CD5+, CD23-, FMC7+), Follicular Lymphoma (CD10+, CD23-), and Hairy Cell Leukemia (pancytopenia, hairy cells, CD11c+, CD25+, CD103+).

<a id='question-040'></a>

### Question 040: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-40 (and chronic inflammatory processes.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the characteristic cytogenetic abnormality (t(15;17)) and its molecular consequence (PML-RARA fusion protein) in Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), and its implication for diagnosis and targeted therapy. (理解急性早幼粒细胞白血病 (APL) 的特征性细胞遗传学异常 (t(15;17)) 及其分子后果 (PML-RARA 融合蛋白)，以及其对诊断和靶向治疗的影响。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fatigue, easy bruising, and recurrent epistaxis. He denies fever or night sweats. Physical examination reveals pallor, multiple petechiae on the upper extremities, and mild splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows: WBC 45,000/µL (normal 4,000-11,000/µL) with 85% blasts, Hgb 8 g/dL (normal 13.5-17.5 g/dL), and platelets 15,000/µL (normal 150,000-450,000/µL). A peripheral blood smear shows numerous abnormal promyelocytes with coarse Auer rods. Bone marrow aspirate confirms the diagnosis of acute myeloid leukemia (AML). Cytogenetic analysis reveals t(15;17)(q24;q21). Which of the following molecular events is directly responsible for the pathogenesis of this patient's leukemia?

(A) Formation of the BCR-ABL fusion protein, leading to constitutive tyrosine kinase activity.
(B) Formation of the PML-RARA fusion protein, disrupting normal retinoic acid signaling and myeloid differentiation.
(C) Formation of the ETV6-RUNX1 fusion protein, blocking myeloid differentiation at the pre-B cell stage.
(D) Formation of the MLL-AF4 fusion protein, leading to aberrant histone modification and leukemogenesis.
(E) Formation of the NPM1 mutation, resulting in cytoplasmic mislocalization and impaired p53 function.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 32-year-old male with fatigue, bruising, epistaxis (suggesting thrombocytopenia and coagulopathy).
- **Physical Exam:** Pallor (anemia), petechiae (thrombocytopenia), mild splenomegaly (common in leukemia).
- **CBC:** High WBC count (leukocytosis) with a predominance of blasts (85%), anemia (low Hgb), and severe thrombocytopenia (low platelets). This points towards acute leukemia.
- **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Abnormal promyelocytes with Auer rods are highly characteristic of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL).
- **Bone Marrow:** Confirms AML.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(15;17)(q24;q21) is the specific translocation associated with APL.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular event directly responsible for the pathogenesis given the clinical and cytogenetic findings.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The translocation t(15;17)(q24;q21) involves the PML gene on chromosome 15 and the RARA (retinoic acid receptor alpha) gene on chromosome 17.
- This translocation results in the fusion of the N-terminal transactivation domain of the PML protein with the C-terminal ligand-binding domain of the RARA protein, creating the PML-RARA fusion protein.
- The PML-RARA fusion protein acts as an aberrant transcription factor. It binds to retinoic acid response elements (RAREs) on DNA but does not require retinoic acid for binding.
- This abnormal binding recruits corepressor complexes (like NCoR and SMRT) to RAREs, leading to histone deacetylation and chromatin condensation.
- This process effectively silences genes required for myeloid differentiation, particularly those normally activated by retinoic acid.
- The result is a block in myeloid differentiation at the promyelocyte stage, leading to the accumulation of these immature cells (promyelocytes) in the bone marrow and peripheral blood, characteristic of APL.
- Importantly, APL is uniquely sensitive to treatment with all-trans retinoic acid (ATRA), which binds to the PML-RARA fusion protein, displaces the corepressors, and allows normal differentiation to resume.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Formation of the BCR-ABL fusion protein is characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and some cases of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL). It results from the t(9;22) translocation (Philadelphia chromosome) and leads to constitutive tyrosine kinase activity, driving uncontrolled cell proliferation. This is incorrect for APL.
- (C): Formation of the ETV6-RUNX1 fusion protein (also known as TEL-AML1) is characteristic of a subtype of childhood ALL, specifically ETV6-RUNX1 positive ALL. It results from the t(12;21) translocation and blocks B-cell differentiation. This is incorrect for APL, which is an AML.
- (D): Formation of the MLL-AF4 fusion protein is associated with a subtype of infant ALL and some cases of AML. It results from the t(4;11) translocation involving the MLL (mixed lineage leukemia) gene and leads to aberrant histone methylation and leukemogenesis. This is incorrect for APL.
- (E): Mutations in the NPM1 gene are common in AML, particularly in cases without specific cytogenetic abnormalities (cytogenetically normal AML, CN-AML). NPM1 is a nucleolar protein involved in ribosome biogenesis and p53 regulation. Mutations often lead to cytoplasmic mislocalization and impaired p53 function, contributing to leukemogenesis. While common in AML, it is not the specific molecular event caused by the t(15;17) translocation seen in this patient.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | APL (t(15;17))                               | CML (t(9;22))                                  | ETV6-RUNX1 ALL (t(12;21))                     | MLL-AF4 ALL/AML (t(4;11))                     | NPM1 mutated AML (common in CN-AML)          |
|----------------------|----------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|
| **Cytogenetic Abnormality** | t(15;17)                                     | t(9;22)                                         | t(12;21)                                        | t(4;11)                                        | Variable (often cytogenetically normal)       |
| **Fusion Protein**   | PML-RARA                                      | BCR-ABL                                         | ETV6-RUNX1                                      | MLL-AF4                                        | N/A (Mutation)                               |
| **Affected Cell Line** | Myeloid (Promyelocyte)                        | Myeloid                                         | Lymphoid (B-cell precursor)                     | Lymphoid (B-cell precursor) / Myeloid           | Myeloid                                      |
| **Key Pathophysiology**| Blocked myeloid differentiation; RA signaling disruption | Constitutive tyrosine kinase activity; uncontrolled proliferation | Blocked B-cell differentiation                | Aberrant histone modification; leukemogenesis | Impaired p53 function; ribosome biogenesis |
| **Characteristic Morphology** | Auer rods in promyelocytes                 | Mature granulocytes, basophils, blasts         | Pre-B ALL morphology                            | ALL/AML morphology                             | Variable AML morphology                      |
| **Specific Treatment** | ATRA, Arsenic Trioxide (ATO)                 | Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors (e.g., Imatinib)     | Standard ALL chemotherapy                      | Standard ALL/AML chemotherapy                  | Standard AML chemotherapy                    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The translocation t(15;17) in APL creates the PML-RARA fusion protein, which disrupts normal myeloid differentiation by aberrantly repressing differentiation genes. This is a key example of how specific cytogenetic abnormalities lead to characteristic leukemias with unique molecular mechanisms and targeted therapies (like ATRA for APL). Differentiating APL from other AML subtypes and leukemias relies on recognizing its specific morphology (Auer rods in promyelocytes), cytogenetics (t(15;17)), and molecular consequence (PML-RARA).

<a id='question-041'></a>

### Question 041: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-41 (Microbiology)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the pathogenesis of Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV)-associated lymphoproliferative disorders, specifically Burkitt lymphoma, including the characteristic genetic translocation and its role in MYC dysregulation. (理解EBV相关淋巴增生性疾病的病理发生机制，特别是伯基特淋巴瘤，包括其特征性基因易位以及MYC失调中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 7-year-old boy from a rural area in equatorial Africa presents with a rapidly enlarging jaw mass and abdominal distension over the past 2 weeks. He has experienced intermittent fevers and night sweats. Physical examination reveals a significant swelling of the maxilla, lymphadenopathy in the cervical and inguinal regions, and hepatosplenomegaly. His vital signs show a temperature of 38.5°C (101.3°F), heart rate of 110 bpm, respiratory rate of 24 breaths/min, and blood pressure of 100/60 mmHg. Laboratory tests show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) at 800 U/L (normal range: 100-250 U/L), hyperuricemia (uric acid 9 mg/dL, normal range: 3.5-7.2 mg/dL), and anemia (hemoglobin 9 g/dL, normal range: 11.5-15.5 g/dL). A biopsy of the jaw mass reveals a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are negative for CD20 and CD10, but strongly positive for CD45 and Ki-67 (proliferation index >90%). Cytogenetic analysis shows a translocation involving chromosomes 8 and 14, t(8;14)(q24;q32).

What is the most likely underlying molecular mechanism driving the malignant transformation in this patient?

(A) Fusion of the *BCL2* gene on chromosome 18 with the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14, leading to overexpression of an anti-apoptotic protein.
(B) Translocation of the *MYC* oncogene on chromosome 8 to the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14, resulting in constitutive overexpression of MYC.
(C) Insertion of viral DNA encoding the *v-MYC* homolog into the host genome, leading to MYC protein production.
(D) Activation of the *RAS* oncogene through point mutation, leading to constitutive activation of downstream signaling pathways promoting cell growth and survival.
(E) Deletion of tumor suppressor genes on chromosome 13, such as *RB1* and *TP53*, leading to loss of cell cycle control and apoptosis regulation.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 7-year-old boy from equatorial Africa. This demographic is highly suggestive of endemic Burkitt lymphoma, which is strongly associated with EBV infection.
- **Presentation:** Rapidly enlarging jaw mass, abdominal distension, fever, night sweats, lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly. These are classic symptoms of a high-grade lymphoma, particularly Burkitt lymphoma, known for its rapid growth and involvement of extranodal sites like the jaw and abdomen.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated LDH, hyperuricemia, anemia. These indicate high tumor burden and rapid cell turnover, characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma (tumor lysis syndrome risk).
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, scant cytoplasm ("starry sky" appearance is often described but not explicitly mentioned here). This morphology is typical for Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** CD45 positive (confirms hematopoietic origin), Ki-67 >90% (very high proliferation rate), CD20 negative (atypical for B-cell lymphoma, but can occur, especially in endemic Burkitt lymphoma; CD10 negativity is also atypical but possible), CD10 negative (usually positive in Burkitt lymphoma, but variants exist). The high Ki-67 is a key feature.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(8;14)(q24;q32). This is the hallmark translocation found in approximately 80% of Burkitt lymphoma cases.

The combination of endemic location, rapid presentation, jaw involvement, high LDH, high proliferation rate (Ki-67), and the specific t(8;14) translocation strongly points to Burkitt lymphoma. The question asks for the molecular mechanism driving this transformation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Burkitt Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Burkitt lymphoma is a B-cell lymphoma characterized by a specific chromosomal translocation, most commonly t(8;14)(q24;q32). This translocation juxtaposes the *MYC* oncogene (located on chromosome 8q24) with the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) locus (located on chromosome 14q32).
- **Mechanism of *MYC* Dysregulation:** The IgH locus is highly active in B-cells due to the process of immunoglobulin gene rearrangement. Placing the *MYC* gene under the control of the strong IgH enhancer leads to constitutive and markedly increased transcription of the *MYC* gene.
- **Role of *MYC*:** MYC is a transcription factor that regulates the expression of numerous genes involved in cell growth, proliferation, metabolism, and apoptosis. Its overexpression drives uncontrolled cell proliferation, which is the hallmark of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **EBV Association:** In endemic Burkitt lymphoma, EBV infection is strongly implicated. EBV infects B-cells and contributes to their immortalization and proliferation, potentially facilitating the translocation event or promoting the survival of cells carrying the translocation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Fusion of *BCL2* with the IgH locus, t(14;18)(q32;q21), is characteristic of Follicular Lymphoma, leading to overexpression of the anti-apoptotic protein BCL2 and inhibition of apoptosis. This is incorrect for Burkitt lymphoma.
- (C): Insertion of viral DNA encoding *v-MYC* is characteristic of Avian Sarcoma Viruses (ASVs) and related retroviral oncogenesis, not human Burkitt lymphoma. While EBV is involved, it doesn't insert a *v-MYC* homolog; it promotes *MYC* overexpression via translocation.
- (D): *RAS* oncogene activation (e.g., through point mutations in *KRAS*, *NRAS*, *HRAS*) leads to constitutive activation of the MAPK pathway, promoting cell growth and survival. While *RAS* mutations can occur in various cancers, they are not the primary driver of Burkitt lymphoma. The hallmark is *MYC* dysregulation.
- (E): Deletion of *RB1* (chromosome 13q14) and *TP53* (chromosome 17p13) are common in retinoblastoma and Li-Fraumeni syndrome, respectively, and can occur in other cancers, leading to loss of cell cycle control and apoptosis. While loss of function of these tumor suppressors can contribute to cancer, the specific genetic hallmark of Burkitt lymphoma is the t(8;14) translocation affecting *MYC*.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (Endemic) | Follicular Lymphoma | Avian Sarcoma | Other Lymphomas |
|----------------------|---------------------------|----------------------|---------------|-----------------|
| **Key Translocation** | t(8;14)(q24;q32)          | t(14;18)(q32;q21)    | N/A (Viral)  | Variable        |
| **Gene Involved**    | *MYC*                     | *BCL2*               | *v-MYC*      | Various         |
| **Partner Gene**     | IgH                       | IgH                  | Cellular Gene| Various         |
| **Mechanism**        | *MYC* Overexpression      | BCL2 Overexpression  | *v-MYC* Prod. | Various         |
| **EBV Association**  | Strong                    | Rare                 | No            | Variable        |
| **Typical Age**      | Children/Young Adults     | Adults               | N/A           | Variable        |
| **Clinical**         | Jaw, Abdomen, Rapid Growth| Lymph Nodes, Slow    | N/A           | Variable        |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is characterized by the t(8;14) translocation, leading to *MYC* overexpression and uncontrolled proliferation. This is a key association to remember. Differentiate it from Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18), *BCL2* overexpression) and other lymphomas based on cytogenetics and clinical presentation. Remember the strong association with EBV in endemic Burkitt lymphoma.

<a id='question-042'></a>

### Question 042: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-42 (Infectious mononucleosis, an acute, self-limited disease)
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating infectious mononucleosis (IM) from other causes of lymphocytosis and atypical lymphocytes, specifically focusing on the characteristic morphology and etiology. (区分传染性单核细胞增多症 (IM) 与其他导致淋巴细胞增多症和非典型淋巴细胞的原因，重点关注其特征形态和病因。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 17-year-old male college student presents to the university health center with a 3-day history of fatigue, sore throat, and low-grade fever (38.0°C). He denies cough, rhinorrhea, or significant muscle aches. Physical examination reveals pharyngeal erythema with tonsillar exudates, bilateral cervical lymphadenopathy (tender, approximately 1 cm in diameter), and mild splenomegaly (palpable 2 cm below the left costal margin). A complete blood count (CBC) shows a total white blood cell (WBC) count of 18,000/µL (normal range: 4,000-11,000/µL) with 75% lymphocytes (normal range: 20-40%) and 5% atypical lymphocytes. Peripheral blood smear review confirms the presence of numerous atypical lymphocytes, characterized by abundant basophilic cytoplasm, irregular nuclear contours, and prominent nucleoli. A Monospot test is positive.

Which of the following is the MOST likely underlying cause of this patient's condition?

(A) Cytomegalovirus (CMV) infection
(B) Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL)
(C) Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV) infection
(D) Toxoplasmosis
(E) Hodgkin Lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 17-year-old male college student - classic demographic for infectious mononucleosis (IM).
*   **Symptoms:** Fatigue, sore throat, low-grade fever - typical symptoms of IM.
*   **Physical Exam:** Pharyngeal erythema with exudates, tender cervical lymphadenopathy, mild splenomegaly - classic signs of IM.
*   **CBC:** Leukocytosis (18,000/µL) with marked lymphocytosis (75%) and atypical lymphocytes (5%) - hallmark laboratory findings of IM.
*   **Peripheral Smear:** Confirms atypical lymphocytes with characteristic morphology (abundant basophilic cytoplasm, irregular nuclei, prominent nucleoli) - strongly suggestive of IM.
*   **Monospot Test:** Positive - specific test for heterophile antibodies produced in response to EBV infection, confirming the diagnosis of IM.

These clues collectively point towards infectious mononucleosis, most commonly caused by Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV).

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Infectious Mononucleosis (IM):** An acute, self-limited illness primarily affecting adolescents and young adults.
*   **Etiology:** Most commonly caused by Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV), a member of the herpesvirus family (specifically, Human herpesvirus 4). EBV primarily infects B lymphocytes.
*   **Pathogenesis:** EBV infects B cells, leading to their proliferation. This proliferation is controlled by cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs), which recognize EBV-infected B cells. The immune response, particularly the CTL response, causes the characteristic symptoms (sore throat, lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly) and the appearance of atypical lymphocytes (activated T cells) in the peripheral blood.
*   **Diagnosis:** Clinical presentation, CBC with lymphocytosis and atypical lymphocytes, and a positive Monospot test (detects heterophile antibodies) are characteristic. EBV-specific antibody testing can also be used.
*   **Differential Diagnosis:** Other viral infections (CMV, HIV, Toxoplasmosis, adenovirus), bacterial pharyngitis, leukemia, lymphoma.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Cytomegalovirus (CMV) infection:** CMV can cause a mononucleosis-like syndrome, especially in immunocompromised individuals, but it typically presents with less severe pharyngitis and lymphadenopathy compared to EBV IM. While it can cause atypical lymphocytosis, the Monospot test is usually negative in CMV mononucleosis. The patient's classic presentation and positive Monospot test make EBV more likely.
- (B) **Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL):** ALL is a malignancy of lymphoid progenitor cells. It can present with fatigue, fever, lymphadenopathy, and splenomegaly. The CBC often shows leukocytosis with a predominance of blasts (immature lymphocytes) rather than mature atypical lymphocytes. The Monospot test would be negative. The morphology of blasts is distinct from the atypical lymphocytes seen in IM.
- (D) **Toxoplasmosis:** Toxoplasmosis can cause a mononucleosis-like illness, particularly in immunocompetent individuals. Symptoms often include lymphadenopathy (especially posterior cervical) but typically less severe pharyngitis and exudates than EBV IM. Atypical lymphocytes can be present, but the Monospot test is negative. Serological testing for Toxoplasma antibodies is used for diagnosis.
- (E) **Hodgkin Lymphoma:** Hodgkin Lymphoma is a malignancy of B lymphocytes characterized by Reed-Sternberg cells. It typically presents with painless lymphadenopathy (often cervical or supraclavicular), fever, night sweats, and weight loss (B symptoms). While lymphadenopathy is present in this patient, the acute onset, sore throat, exudates, splenomegaly, atypical lymphocytosis, and positive Monospot test are not characteristic of Hodgkin Lymphoma. Diagnosis requires lymph node biopsy.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Infectious Mononucleosis (EBV) | CMV Mononucleosis | ALL                     | Toxoplasmosis         | Hodgkin Lymphoma      |
| :------------------ | :----------------------------- | :---------------- | :---------------------- | :-------------------- | :-------------------- |
| **Age Group**       | Adolescents/Young Adults       | Variable          | Children/Young Adults   | Variable              | Bimodal (Young/Old)   |
| **Symptoms**        | Fever, Sore Throat, Fatigue, LAD | Fever, Fatigue    | Fever, Fatigue, LAD, Bone Pain | Fever, LAD, Fatigue | Fever, Night Sweats, Weight Loss, LAD |
| **Pharyngitis/Exudates** | Common/Prominent              | Less Common       | Uncommon                | Less Common           | Uncommon              |
| **Splenomegaly**    | Common                         | Common            | Common                  | Common                | Uncommon              |
| **CBC**             | Lymphocytosis, Atypical Lymphs | Lymphocytosis     | Leukocytosis (Blasts)   | Lymphocytosis         | Variable              |
| **Monospot Test**   | Positive                       | Negative          | Negative                | Negative              | Negative              |
| **Pathology**       | Atypical Lymphocytes (T cells) | Atypical Lymphs   | Blasts                  | Atypical Lymphs       | Reed-Sternberg Cells  |
| **Etiology**        | EBV                            | CMV               | Malignant Transformation | Toxoplasma gondii     | Malignant B cells    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Infectious mononucleosis, typically caused by EBV, presents classically in adolescents with fever, pharyngitis, lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly, and characteristic atypical lymphocytosis. A positive Monospot test strongly supports this diagnosis. Differentiating IM from other causes of mononucleosis-like syndromes (CMV, Toxoplasmosis) relies on clinical features, atypical lymphocyte morphology, and specific serological tests (Monospot for EBV, CMV IgM/IgG, Toxoplasma IgM/IgG). Malignancies like ALL and Hodgkin Lymphoma must also be considered in the differential, especially if symptoms are atypical or persistent, but they lack the characteristic features of IM (e.g., positive Monospot, specific atypical lymphocyte morphology).

<a id='question-043'></a>

### Question 043: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-43 (usually resolves in 4–6 weeks, is an example of a )
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the clinical presentation, cytogenetics, and characteristic features of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and differentiating it from other myeloproliferative neoplasms or reactive leukocytosis. (理解慢性粒细胞白血病 (CML) 的临床表现、细胞遗传学和特征，并将其与其他骨髓增殖性肿瘤或反应性白细胞增多症区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and early satiety for the past 2 months. He denies fever, weight loss, or bone pain. Physical examination reveals mild splenomegaly. A complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell (WBC) count of 180 x 10^9/L (normal range: 4-11 x 10^9/L) with a left shift, including immature granulocytes (myelocytes, metamyelocytes, bands). Hemoglobin is 11 g/dL (normal range: 13.5-17.5 g/dL), and platelet count is 600 x 10^9/L (normal range: 150-400 x 10^9/L). A peripheral blood smear shows a marked increase in granulocytes at all stages of maturation. Bone marrow aspiration and biopsy are performed. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome), t(9;22)(q34;q11). Which of the following molecular abnormalities is directly responsible for the constitutive activation of tyrosine kinase activity in this patient's condition?

(A) Mutation in the *JAK2* gene leading to V617F substitution.
(B) Fusion gene resulting from the translocation t(9;22)(q34;q11).
(C) Overexpression of the *BCL2* gene due to t(14;18)(q32;q21).
(D) Mutation in the *FLT3* gene leading to internal tandem duplication (ITD).
(E) Amplification of the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male. Age is typical for CML presentation.
- **Symptoms:** Fatigue, night sweats, early satiety (due to splenomegaly). These are common constitutional symptoms associated with CML.
- **Physical Exam:** Mild splenomegaly. Splenomegaly is a hallmark finding in CML due to extramedullary hematopoiesis and sequestration of leukemic cells.
- **CBC:** Markedly elevated WBC count (180 x 10^9/L) with a left shift (immature granulocytes present). This indicates a myeloproliferative disorder. Anemia (Hgb 11 g/dL) and thrombocytosis (platelets 600 x 10^9/L) are also common in CML.
- **Peripheral Smear:** Increased granulocytes at all stages of maturation. This "full spectrum" of granulocyte maturation is characteristic of CML.
- **Cytogenetics:** Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome), t(9;22)(q34;q11). This is the defining cytogenetic abnormality of CML.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular abnormality responsible for constitutive tyrosine kinase activity. This directly links the Ph chromosome to its molecular consequence.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome) results from a reciprocal translocation between the long arms of chromosomes 9 and 22, denoted as t(9;22)(q34;q11).
- This translocation fuses the *ABL1* gene (Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1) from chromosome 9 with the *BCR* gene (breakpoint cluster region) on chromosome 22.
- The resulting fusion gene, *BCR-ABL1*, encodes a constitutively active tyrosine kinase protein (p210 BCR-ABL).
- This aberrant tyrosine kinase activity drives uncontrolled proliferation of myeloid progenitor cells, leading to the characteristic features of CML: leukocytosis with a left shift, splenomegaly, and potential progression to blast crisis.
- The question asks for the *molecular abnormality* responsible for the *constitutive activation of tyrosine kinase activity*. The *BCR-ABL1* fusion gene is the direct cause of this activity.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Mutation in the *JAK2* gene leading to V617F substitution. This mutation is characteristic of Polycythemia Vera (PV), Essential Thrombocythemia (ET), and Primary Myelofibrosis (PMF), which are other myeloproliferative neoplasms. While these conditions can present with elevated blood counts, the Ph chromosome is absent, and the underlying molecular mechanism involves constitutive activation of JAK-STAT signaling, not BCR-ABL tyrosine kinase.
- (C): Overexpression of the *BCL2* gene due to t(14;18)(q32;q21). This translocation is characteristic of Follicular Lymphoma, a B-cell lymphoma. It leads to overexpression of the anti-apoptotic protein BCL2, promoting lymphocyte survival. This is unrelated to CML and myeloid proliferation.
- (D): Mutation in the *FLT3* gene leading to internal tandem duplication (ITD). *FLT3* mutations are common in Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML), particularly those with normal cytogenetics. They lead to constitutive activation of the FLT3 receptor tyrosine kinase, promoting leukemic cell proliferation. While it involves tyrosine kinase activation, it's specific to AML, not CML, and the genetic abnormality is different.
- (E): Amplification of the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8. *MYC* amplification is often seen in Burkitt Lymphoma, a high-grade B-cell lymphoma. It drives cell proliferation but is not the specific mechanism in CML.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) | Polycythemia Vera (PV) | Follicular Lymphoma | Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML) | Burkitt Lymphoma |
|----------------------|---------------------------------|--------------------------|----------------------|------------------------------|-----------------|
| **Key Genetic Abnormality** | t(9;22)(q34;q11) (Ph Chromosome) | *JAK2* V617F mutation   | t(14;18)(q32;q21)    | *FLT3* ITD (common)         | *MYC* Amplification |
| **Primary Cell Line** | Myeloid Progenitors             | Erythroid Progenitors    | B-Lymphocytes        | Myeloid Progenitors          | B-Lymphocytes   |
| **Hallmark Protein** | BCR-ABL1 Tyrosine Kinase        | JAK2 Tyrosine Kinase     | BCL2 Anti-apoptotic  | FLT3 Tyrosine Kinase         | MYC Transcription Factor |
| **Typical Presentation** | Fatigue, Splenomegaly, Leukocytosis w/ Left Shift | Erythrocytosis, Thrombocytosis, Pruritus | Lymphadenopathy, indolent | Fatigue, Bleeding, Infection | Rapidly growing mass |
| **Constitutive Activity** | Tyrosine Kinase                | JAK-STAT Pathway         | Anti-apoptosis       | Tyrosine Kinase              | Transcription |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The Philadelphia chromosome, t(9;22), creates the *BCR-ABL1* fusion gene, which encodes a constitutively active tyrosine kinase responsible for the pathogenesis of CML. Differentiating CML from other myeloproliferative neoplasms (like PV, ET, PMF) relies on recognizing the Ph chromosome and the specific molecular target (BCR-ABL). Key differentials include other leukemias (AML, ALL) and lymphomas, each with distinct genetic drivers (e.g., *FLT3* in AML, *BCL2* in Follicular Lymphoma, *MYC* in Burkitt Lymphoma) and clinical presentations.

---

<a id='question-044'></a>

### Question 044: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-44 (causes lymphocytosis.The most common cause is Epst)
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating between various causes of lymphocytosis, specifically distinguishing between reactive lymphocytosis (e.g., infectious mononucleosis) and neoplastic lymphocytosis (e.g., chronic lymphocytic leukemia), based on clinical presentation, peripheral blood smear findings, and immunophenotyping. (区分淋巴细胞增多症的各种原因，特别是基于临床表现、外周血涂片发现和免疫表型分析，区分反应性淋巴细胞增多症（例如传染性单核细胞增多症）和新生物性淋巴细胞增多症（例如慢性淋巴细胞白血病）。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to his primary care physician for a routine check-up. He reports feeling generally well but has noticed increased fatigue over the past few months. He denies fever, night sweats, weight loss, or recent infections. His past medical history is unremarkable, and he takes no medications. Vital signs are stable. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and axillary regions, and mild splenomegaly. Laboratory studies show a white blood cell count of 28,000/µL (normal range: 4,000-11,000/µL) with a differential showing 85% lymphocytes (normal range: 20-40%). Peripheral blood smear reveals numerous small, mature-appearing lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and scant cytoplasm. Flow cytometry on the peripheral blood lymphocytes shows positivity for CD5, CD19, CD20 (dim), and CD23, and negativity for CD10 and FMC7. Karyotyping reveals a deletion in the short arm of chromosome 13 (del(13q)).

What is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Infectious mononucleosis
(B) Hairy cell leukemia
(C) Follicular lymphoma
(D) Chronic lymphocytic leukemia
(E) Mantle cell lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Demographics:** 62-year-old male. This age group is typical for chronic lymphoproliferative disorders like CLL.
*   **Symptoms:** Fatigue, generalized lymphadenopathy, mild splenomegaly. These are common, often indolent symptoms associated with CLL. Absence of B symptoms (fever, night sweats, weight loss) suggests a less aggressive process.
*   **Laboratory Findings:** Marked lymphocytosis (WBC 28,000/µL, 85% lymphocytes). This points towards either a reactive process or a lymphoid malignancy.
*   **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Small, mature-appearing lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and scant cytoplasm. This morphology is characteristic of CLL cells (also known as smudge cells when fragile cells rupture during smear preparation, though not explicitly mentioned here, the description fits).
*   **Flow Cytometry:** CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+, CD10-, FMC7-. This immunophenotype is highly specific for CLL/SLL (Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma). CD5 is typically expressed on T cells but aberrantly on CLL B cells. CD23 is also characteristically positive in CLL. CD10 and FMC7 are typically negative.
*   **Karyotyping:** del(13q). This is the most common cytogenetic abnormality found in CLL and is associated with a relatively favorable prognosis.

These findings collectively point strongly towards Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL).

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **CLL Pathophysiology:** CLL is a clonal B-cell malignancy characterized by the accumulation of functionally incompetent, mature-appearing lymphocytes in the blood, bone marrow, and lymphoid tissues. The malignant cells express B-cell markers (CD19, CD20) and the T-cell marker CD5. They are resistant to apoptosis and proliferate independently of normal growth factors.
*   **Molecular Basis:** CLL is associated with various genetic abnormalities, including deletions (del(13q), del(11q), del(17p)), trisomy 12, and point mutations (e.g., in *IGHV*, *TP53*, *SF3B1*, *NOTCH1*). del(13q) is the most frequent abnormality (~55%) and is generally associated with a better prognosis compared to del(11q) or del(17p).
*   **Immunophenotype:** The characteristic immunophenotype (CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+) is crucial for diagnosis and differentiation from other lymphoproliferative disorders. The dim expression of CD20 is due to shedding of the CD20 molecule from the cell surface.
*   **Clinical Course:** CLL typically follows an indolent course, with many patients remaining asymptomatic for years. Treatment is usually initiated when symptoms develop or disease progresses based on specific criteria (e.g., Rai or Binet staging).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Infectious mononucleosis:** Caused primarily by Epstein-Barr virus (EBV). Typically presents in adolescents/young adults with fever, pharyngitis, lymphadenopathy, and fatigue. Peripheral blood shows atypical lymphocytes (larger, more irregular nuclei, abundant cytoplasm) and often transient lymphocytosis. Flow cytometry would show polyclonal B cells, not the specific CLL phenotype. The patient's age and the morphology/immunophenotype of the lymphocytes are inconsistent with infectious mononucleosis.
- (B) **Hairy cell leukemia:** A rare B-cell leukemia characterized by pancytopenia (not lymphocytosis), splenomegaly, and the presence of "hairy cells" on peripheral smear (cells with cytoplasmic projections). Immunophenotype is typically CD19+, CD20+, CD11c+, CD25+, CD103+, CD5-, CD23-. The patient's presentation (lymphocytosis, specific immunophenotype) is inconsistent with hairy cell leukemia.
- (C) **Follicular lymphoma:** A common indolent B-cell lymphoma typically presenting with painless lymphadenopathy. Peripheral blood involvement can occur but usually doesn't cause marked lymphocytosis with the characteristic CLL morphology. Immunophenotype is typically CD19+, CD20+, CD10+, BCL2+, CD5-, CD23-. The CD10 positivity and lack of CD5 are key distinguishing features from CLL.
- (E) **Mantle cell lymphoma:** An aggressive B-cell lymphoma. Can present with lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly, and sometimes leukemic involvement. Immunophenotype is typically CD19+, CD20+, CD5+, Cyclin D1+, CD23-. While it shares CD5 positivity with CLL, it is typically CD23 negative and strongly positive for Cyclin D1 (due to t(11;14) translocation), which is not mentioned in the vignette but is a key differentiator. The morphology (often slightly larger cells than CLL) and clinical course are also usually more aggressive than described.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL) | Infectious Mononucleosis | Hairy Cell Leukemia | Follicular Lymphoma | Mantle Cell Lymphoma |
| :------------------ | :--------------------------------- | :----------------------- | :------------------ | :------------------ | :------------------- |
| **Age**             | Older adults (>60)                 | Adolescents/Young Adults | Middle-aged/Older   | Variable            | Middle-aged/Older    |
| **WBC Count**       | High (Lymphocytosis)               | High (Lymphocytosis)    | Low (Leukopenia)    | Variable            | Variable             |
| **Lymphocyte Morphology** | Small, mature, clumped chromatin   | Atypical lymphocytes    | Hairy cells         | Variable            | Variable             |
| **CD5**             | Positive                           | Negative                 | Negative            | Negative            | Positive             |
| **CD10**            | Negative                           | Negative                 | Negative            | Positive            | Negative             |
| **CD23**            | Positive                           | Negative                 | Negative            | Negative            | Negative             |
| **Cyclin D1**       | Negative                           | Negative                 | Negative            | Negative            | Positive             |
| **Cytogenetics**    | del(13q) common                    | N/A                      | N/A                 | N/A                 | t(11;14) common      |
| **Clinical Course** | Indolent                           | Self-limited             | Indolent            | Indolent            | Aggressive           |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CLL is the most common leukemia in adults in Western countries, characterized by marked lymphocytosis with small, mature lymphocytes and a specific immunophenotype (CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+). Differentiating CLL from reactive lymphocytosis (like infectious mononucleosis) and other lymphoid malignancies requires careful evaluation of clinical presentation, morphology, immunophenotyping, and cytogenetics. Remember the key immunophenotype markers for CLL (CD5+, CD23+) and how they differ from other B-cell lymphomas like Follicular Lymphoma (CD10+, CD23-) and Mantle Cell Lymphoma (CD23-, Cyclin D1+).

<a id='question-045'></a>

### Question 045: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-45 ((a herpesvirus) though other virusescan cause it a)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the pathogenesis, histopathology, and molecular features of Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV)-associated lymphoproliferative disorders, specifically Burkitt lymphoma, and differentiating it from other lymphomas and leukemias. (理解EBV相关淋巴增生性疾病的发病机制、组织病理学和分子特征，特别是伯基特淋巴瘤，并将其与其他淋巴瘤和白血病区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 7-year-old boy from a rural area in equatorial Africa presents with a rapidly enlarging abdominal mass and facial swelling that has developed over the past 2 weeks. He has also experienced intermittent fevers and night sweats. Physical examination reveals a large, firm, non-tender mass in the right lower quadrant, hepatosplenomegaly, and bilateral parotid gland enlargement. Laboratory studies show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) levels (1500 U/L, normal <250 U/L), hyperuricemia (10 mg/dL, normal <7 mg/dL), and anemia (hemoglobin 9 g/dL, normal 11.5-15.5 g/dL). A biopsy of the abdominal mass shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are negative for CD20 and CD10, but strongly positive for CD45 and Ki-67 (proliferation index >90%). Immunohistochemistry reveals positivity for c-myc protein.

(A) The underlying mechanism involves translocation of the *BCL2* gene, leading to overexpression of an anti-apoptotic protein.
(B) The characteristic histopathology includes Reed-Sternberg cells surrounded by a mixed inflammatory infiltrate.
(C) The most likely diagnosis is Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL), characterized by the proliferation of immature lymphoid precursors (lymphoblasts) in the bone marrow and peripheral blood.
(D) The underlying genetic abnormality is a translocation involving the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8, typically t(8;14), leading to its constitutive overexpression.
(E) The tumor cells typically express CD5 and are positive for cyclin D1, consistent with Mantle Cell Lymphoma.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 7-year-old boy from equatorial Africa. This demographic is highly suggestive of endemic Burkitt lymphoma, which is strongly associated with EBV infection in this region.
*   **Symptoms:** Rapidly enlarging abdominal mass, facial swelling (jaw involvement), fever, night sweats. These are classic symptoms of a high-grade lymphoma, particularly Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Physical Exam:** Abdominal mass, hepatosplenomegaly, parotid gland enlargement. These findings point towards widespread lymphoproliferation.
*   **Labs:** Elevated LDH (tumor marker for high cell turnover), hyperuricemia (due to rapid cell lysis), anemia (bone marrow involvement or chronic disease). These are characteristic laboratory findings in Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm ("starry sky" appearance is often seen but not explicitly mentioned here). This morphology is highly characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** CD45+ (leukocyte marker), Ki-67 >90% (very high proliferation rate), CD20- (B-cell marker, often lost or weak in Burkitt), CD10- (B-cell marker, often lost or weak in Burkitt), c-myc positive. The strong positivity for c-myc is a key feature.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the underlying mechanism.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Burkitt Lymphoma:** A highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma. Endemic form (Africa) is strongly linked to EBV and malaria. Sporadic form (elsewhere) is less associated with EBV. Immunodeficiency-associated form occurs in HIV patients.
*   **Pathogenesis:** EBV infects B cells. In endemic Burkitt lymphoma, chronic malaria infection impairs the immune response to EBV-infected B cells, leading to their accumulation. These B cells undergo malignant transformation.
*   **Genetic Hallmark:** The defining genetic event is a translocation involving the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8 (8q24). The most common translocation is t(8;14)(q24;q32), which places *MYC* under the control of the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) enhancer on chromosome 14. Other translocations involve the kappa light chain locus (t(2;8)) or the lambda light chain locus (t(8;22)).
*   **Molecular Mechanism:** The translocation leads to constitutive overexpression of the c-Myc protein, a transcription factor that regulates cell growth, proliferation, apoptosis, and metabolism. This drives the rapid proliferation characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Histopathology:** "Starry sky" appearance due to numerous interspersed macrophages phagocytosing apoptotic tumor cells. Cells are medium-sized with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, prominent nucleoli, and high mitotic rate.
*   **Immunophenotype:** Typically B-cell lineage (CD19+, CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+), but CD20 and CD10 expression can be variable or lost. High Ki-67 index (>90%). c-Myc overexpression is characteristic.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** Translocation of the *BCL2* gene (typically t(14;18)) is characteristic of Follicular Lymphoma, leading to overexpression of the anti-apoptotic protein BCL2 and inhibition of apoptosis. This is not the mechanism for Burkitt lymphoma.
- (B): **Incorrect.** Reed-Sternberg cells are the hallmark of Hodgkin Lymphoma, not Burkitt lymphoma. Hodgkin lymphoma typically presents with lymphadenopathy, often cervical, and has a different immunophenotype (CD30+, CD15+).
- (C): **Incorrect.** Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL) involves the proliferation of immature lymphoid precursors (lymphoblasts) primarily in the bone marrow and peripheral blood, often leading to pancytopenia. While lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly can occur, the primary site of involvement and the morphology/immunophenotype are different from Burkitt lymphoma. Burkitt lymphoma is primarily a solid tumor mass, although leukemic phase can occur.
- (E): **Incorrect.** Expression of CD5 and cyclin D1 is characteristic of Mantle Cell Lymphoma, which results from the t(11;14) translocation involving the *CCND1* gene (encoding cyclin D1). Mantle Cell Lymphoma typically affects older adults and has a different morphology and clinical presentation.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (Endemic) | Follicular Lymphoma | Hodgkin Lymphoma | ALL                     | Mantle Cell Lymphoma |
| :------------------ | :------------------------ | :------------------ | :--------------- | :---------------------- | :------------------- |
| **Age**             | Children/Young Adults     | Middle-Aged/Elderly | Bimodal (Young & Elderly) | Children/Young Adults | Middle-Aged/Elderly  |
| **Location**        | Jaw, Abdomen              | Lymph Nodes         | Lymph Nodes      | Bone Marrow/Blood      | Lymph Nodes/Spleen   |
| **EBV Association** | Strong (Endemic)          | Weak                | Weak             | Rare                    | Rare                 |
| **Histology**       | Medium cells, "Starry Sky" | Follicular pattern  | Reed-Sternberg cells | Lymphoblasts            | Small lymphocytes    |
| **Key Marker**      | c-Myc                     | BCL2                | CD30, CD15       | TdT, CD19/CD10 (pre-B) | Cyclin D1, CD5       |
| **Genetic Abnorm.** | t(8;14) (*MYC*)            | t(14;18) (*BCL2*)    | Rare             | Various (e.g., t(9;22)) | t(11;14) (*CCND1*)   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is a high-grade B-cell lymphoma characterized by *MYC* translocation (most commonly t(8;14)), leading to c-Myc overexpression and rapid proliferation. It is strongly associated with EBV in endemic regions (Africa) and presents with rapidly growing masses, often involving the jaw or abdomen, and characteristic laboratory findings (high LDH, hyperuricemia). Differentiating it from other lymphomas requires understanding their specific genetic translocations, histopathology, and immunophenotypes. Key differentials include Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18), BCL2), Hodgkin Lymphoma (Reed-Sternberg cells), ALL (lymphoblasts), and Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(11;14), Cyclin D1, CD5).

<a id='question-046'></a>

### Question 046: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-46 (negative infectious mononucleosisis most likely du)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating between various lymphoid neoplasms based on morphology, immunophenotype, and genetic features, specifically distinguishing Burkitt lymphoma from other aggressive lymphomas and reactive lymphoid proliferations. (区分各种淋巴瘤的新生，基于形态学、免疫表型和遗传学特征，特别是区分伯基特淋巴瘤和其他侵袭性淋巴瘤以及反应性淋巴细胞增殖。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 25-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of rapidly enlarging abdominal mass, intermittent fever, and night sweats. He denies recent travel, insect bites, or exposure to sick individuals. Physical examination reveals a large, palpable mass in the lower abdomen, hepatosplenomegaly, and cervical lymphadenopathy. Vital signs show a temperature of 38.5°C (101.3°F), heart rate of 110 bpm, blood pressure of 120/80 mmHg, and respiratory rate of 18 breaths/min. Laboratory studies show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) at 800 U/L (normal range: 100-250 U/L), hyperuricemia (uric acid 9.0 mg/dL, normal range: 3.5-7.0 mg/dL), and anemia (hemoglobin 9.5 g/dL, normal range: 13.5-17.5 g/dL). A CT scan of the abdomen and pelvis confirms a large retroperitoneal mass encasing the aorta and inferior vena cava. An excisional biopsy of a cervical lymph node is performed. Histopathology shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are arranged in sheets with interspersed macrophages, some containing phagocytosed cellular debris ("tingible body macrophages"). Immunohistochemistry reveals the tumor cells are positive for CD20, CD10, BCL6, and MUM1, and negative for CD5, BCL2, and TdT. Cytogenetic analysis shows a translocation t(8;14)(q24;q32). Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) testing is positive.

Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL)
(B) Follicular Lymphoma
(C) Burkitt Lymphoma
(D) Hodgkin Lymphoma
(E) Mantle Cell Lymphoma

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** Young adult male (25 years old).
*   **Presentation:** Rapidly enlarging abdominal mass, fever, night sweats (B symptoms), hepatosplenomegaly, lymphadenopathy. This suggests an aggressive lymphoma.
*   **Labs:** Elevated LDH and uric acid indicate high cell turnover and tumor lysis, common in aggressive lymphomas. Anemia is also present.
*   **Imaging:** Large retroperitoneal mass encasing major vessels suggests aggressive local invasion.
*   **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells, round nuclei, clumped chromatin, small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm, "starry sky" appearance (sheets of tumor cells with interspersed tingible body macrophages). This morphology is highly characteristic.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+ (B-cell marker), CD10+ (germinal center marker), BCL6+ (germinal center marker), MUM1+ (plasma cell differentiation marker, often seen in Burkitt), CD5- (rules out CLL/SLL, Mantle Cell Lymphoma), BCL2- (rules out Follicular Lymphoma), TdT- (rules out lymphoblastic lymphoma/leukemia).
*   **Cytogenetics:** t(8;14)(q24;q32) is the hallmark translocation involving the MYC oncogene and the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus.
*   **EBV:** Positive EBV association is common in Burkitt lymphoma, especially the endemic form.

These features collectively point strongly towards Burkitt lymphoma. The rapid growth, high LDH/uric acid, "starry sky" morphology, specific immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2-), t(8;14) translocation, and EBV association are all classic for Burkitt lymphoma.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Burkitt Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma characterized by a specific chromosomal translocation, most commonly t(8;14)(q24;q32). This translocation juxtaposes the MYC oncogene (located on chromosome 8q24) with immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) gene enhancers (on chromosome 14q32). This leads to constitutive overexpression of the MYC protein, a transcription factor that drives cell proliferation, metabolism, and apoptosis inhibition. The resulting cells proliferate rapidly, leading to the clinical presentation.
*   **Morphology:** The characteristic "starry sky" appearance is due to the numerous interspersed macrophages (tingible body macrophages) phagocytosing apoptotic tumor cells.
*   **Immunophenotype:** Burkitt lymphoma cells are B-cells (CD20+) that typically express germinal center markers like CD10 and BCL6. They often express MUM1 (a plasma cell marker) and lack BCL2 expression (unlike Follicular Lymphoma).
*   **Genetic Basis:** The MYC translocation is the defining genetic event. Variants include t(2;8) and t(8;22), involving the kappa light chain or lambda light chain locus, respectively, instead of the IgH locus.
*   **EBV Association:** EBV infection is strongly associated with Burkitt lymphoma, particularly the endemic form found in equatorial Africa. EBV provides signals that promote B-cell proliferation and survival, potentially contributing to the development of the lymphoma in genetically susceptible individuals.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL):** DLBCL is the most common type of non-Hodgkin lymphoma. It is also aggressive but typically lacks the specific morphology ("starry sky"), immunophenotype (often CD10-, BCL2+, MUM1-), and specific t(8;14) translocation seen in this case. While DLBCL can present similarly, the constellation of findings here is much more specific for Burkitt lymphoma. DLBCL is more heterogeneous and lacks the consistent genetic hallmark.
- (B) **Follicular Lymphoma:** Follicular lymphoma is typically an indolent (slow-growing) lymphoma, although it can transform into a more aggressive form. Histologically, it shows a follicular growth pattern (nodular). Immunophenotypically, it is characterized by BCL2 overexpression due to the t(14;18) translocation, which is absent in this case. The clinical presentation (rapidly enlarging mass, high LDH) is also less typical for untreated follicular lymphoma.
- (D) **Hodgkin Lymphoma:** Hodgkin lymphoma is characterized by the presence of Reed-Sternberg cells (large, multinucleated cells) in a background of reactive inflammatory cells. The morphology described (medium-sized lymphoid cells, "starry sky") is not typical for Hodgkin lymphoma. Immunophenotypically, Reed-Sternberg cells are typically CD30+ and CD15+, and lack B-cell markers like CD20 (though some variants exist). The t(8;14) translocation is not associated with Hodgkin lymphoma.
- (E) **Mantle Cell Lymphoma:** Mantle cell lymphoma is a B-cell lymphoma characterized by the t(11;14) translocation involving the CCND1 gene (cyclin D1). Immunophenotypically, it is typically CD5+ and expresses cyclin D1. The morphology and immunophenotype described in the vignette (CD10+, BCL2-, MUM1+) are inconsistent with Mantle Cell Lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma          | DLBCL                     | Follicular Lymphoma       | Hodgkin Lymphoma         | Mantle Cell Lymphoma     |
| :------------------ | :------------------------ | :------------------------ | :------------------------ | :------------------------ | :------------------------ |
| **Clinical**        | Rapid growth, B symptoms, high LDH/uric acid | Aggressive, B symptoms | Indolent (usually)       | Variable, B symptoms      | Variable                 |
| **Morphology**      | "Starry sky"              | Diffuse sheets           | Follicular pattern        | Reed-Sternberg cells      | Diffuse/nodular           |
| **Immunophenotype** | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2- | Variable (CD20+, CD10-, BCL2+) | CD20+, CD10+, BCL2+       | RS cells CD30+, CD15+, CD20- | CD20+, CD5+, Cyclin D1+ |
| **Genetics**        | t(8;14), MYC             | Variable                 | t(14;18), BCL2             | None specific             | t(11;14), CCND1           |
| **EBV**             | Often positive            | Variable                 | Rare                      | Variable                 | Rare                      |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell lymphoma characterized by MYC translocation (t(8;14)), rapid proliferation, high LDH/uric acid, "starry sky" morphology, and a specific immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL2-). Key differentials include other aggressive B-cell lymphomas like DLBCL and indolent lymphomas like Follicular Lymphoma, which can be distinguished by morphology, immunophenotype, and genetics. Hodgkin lymphoma is distinct due to Reed-Sternberg cells. Mantle cell lymphoma has a different genetic translocation (t(11;14)) and immunophenotype (CD5+).
