<a id='question-047'></a>

### Question 047: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-47 (virus)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the pathogenesis and characteristic features of Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV)-associated lymphoproliferative disorders, specifically Burkitt lymphoma, including its morphology, immunophenotype, and genetic translocation. (理解EBV相关淋巴增生性疾病的病理发生机制和特征，特别是伯基特淋巴瘤，包括其形态学、免疫表型和基因易位。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 7-year-old boy from a rural area in equatorial Africa presents with a rapidly enlarging mass in his jaw, causing facial swelling and difficulty eating. He also reports intermittent fevers and night sweats for the past month. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender mass involving the mandible, along with generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and inguinal regions. His spleen is palpable 5 cm below the left costal margin. Laboratory studies show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) levels (1500 U/L, normal <250 U/L) and hyperuricemia (10 mg/dL, normal <7 mg/dL). A biopsy of the jaw mass shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are arranged in sheets with interspersed macrophages, some containing phagocytosed cellular debris ("starry sky" appearance). Immunohistochemistry reveals the tumor cells to be positive for CD20, CD10, BCL6, and MUM1, but negative for CD5 and BCL2. Cytogenetic analysis shows a translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8 and the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14, t(8;14)(q24;q32).

Which of the following is the most likely underlying mechanism contributing to the development of this patient's condition?

(A) Viral integration into the host genome, leading to constitutive activation of cellular oncogenes and suppression of tumor suppressor genes.
(B) Chronic immune stimulation leading to polyclonal B-cell proliferation and subsequent malignant transformation.
(C) Aberrant expression of BCL2 protein, inhibiting apoptosis and promoting cell survival.
(D) Dysregulation of the MYC transcription factor due to chromosomal translocation, leading to uncontrolled cell proliferation.
(E) Defective DNA mismatch repair leading to microsatellite instability and accumulation of mutations.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 7-year-old boy from equatorial Africa. This demographic is highly suggestive of endemic Burkitt lymphoma, which is strongly associated with EBV infection.
*   **Presenting Symptoms:** Rapidly enlarging jaw mass, fever, night sweats, lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly. These are classic signs of a high-grade lymphoma. The jaw involvement is particularly characteristic of endemic Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated LDH and hyperuricemia indicate high tumor burden and rapid cell turnover, common in Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with characteristic morphology (round nuclei, clumped chromatin, small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm) and "starry sky" appearance (macrophages phagocytosing apoptotic cells). This is pathognomonic for Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+ profile is typical for Burkitt lymphoma. CD5- and BCL2- help distinguish it from other lymphomas like CLL/SLL or follicular lymphoma.
*   **Cytogenetics:** t(8;14)(q24;q32) is the hallmark genetic translocation found in ~80% of Burkitt lymphoma cases, leading to overexpression of the MYC oncogene.

The question asks for the *underlying mechanism*. The vignette strongly points to Burkitt lymphoma. The key genetic finding is the t(8;14) translocation involving the MYC gene. This translocation places the MYC proto-oncogene under the control of the strong immunoglobulin heavy chain enhancer, leading to its constitutive overexpression. MYC is a transcription factor that regulates genes involved in cell growth, proliferation, and apoptosis. Its overexpression drives uncontrolled cell proliferation, which is the fundamental mechanism underlying Burkitt lymphoma.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Burkitt Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Burkitt lymphoma is a B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma characterized by rapid proliferation. The endemic form, common in equatorial Africa, is strongly associated with EBV infection. EBV infects B lymphocytes and promotes their proliferation. In genetically susceptible individuals, this chronic proliferation can lead to malignant transformation.
*   **Genetic Translocation:** The hallmark genetic abnormality is the t(8;14)(q24;q32) translocation, which juxtaposes the MYC gene (on chromosome 8q24) with the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) locus (on chromosome 14q32). This places MYC under the control of the highly active IgH enhancer, leading to its constitutive overexpression. Variant translocations involving MYC and other immunoglobulin loci (t(2;8) or t(8;22)) can also occur.
*   **MYC Function:** MYC is a transcription factor that regulates the expression of numerous genes involved in cell cycle progression, metabolism, and apoptosis. Overexpression of MYC drives cells into the S phase of the cell cycle, promoting rapid proliferation and inhibiting differentiation. It also sensitizes cells to apoptosis, which contributes to the high turnover rate seen in Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **EBV Role:** EBV infection is a critical factor in the pathogenesis of endemic Burkitt lymphoma. EBV latent membrane protein 1 (LMP1) can mimic CD40 signaling, promoting B-cell survival and proliferation. EBV also expresses other proteins that contribute to B-cell transformation. However, EBV infection alone is not sufficient; the MYC translocation is the defining event leading to malignancy.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Viral integration into the host genome, leading to constitutive activation of cellular oncogenes and suppression of tumor suppressor genes. This mechanism is characteristic of certain viruses like Human T-lymphotropic virus type 1 (HTLV-1) which causes Adult T-cell leukemia/lymphoma via Tax protein activation of NF-κB and integration into the genome. While EBV is involved in Burkitt lymphoma, its primary role is not through direct integration and widespread oncogene activation/suppression like HTLV-1. EBV primarily drives proliferation, creating a field for the MYC translocation to occur.
- (B): Chronic immune stimulation leading to polyclonal B-cell proliferation and subsequent malignant transformation. Chronic immune stimulation (e.g., by EBV or other infections) can lead to polyclonal B-cell expansion. However, Burkitt lymphoma is a *monoclonal* B-cell malignancy driven by a specific genetic event (MYC translocation), not simply polyclonal proliferation. While EBV provides the stimulus, the key mechanism is the MYC deregulation.
- (C): Aberrant expression of BCL2 protein, inhibiting apoptosis and promoting cell survival. Overexpression of BCL2, typically due to the t(14;18) translocation, is the hallmark of follicular lymphoma. While BCL2 is an anti-apoptotic protein, Burkitt lymphoma cells are typically BCL2-negative (or express low levels) and are actually quite sensitive to apoptosis, contributing to their high turnover. The primary driver of proliferation in Burkitt lymphoma is MYC, not BCL2.
- (E): Defective DNA mismatch repair leading to microsatellite instability and accumulation of mutations. Defective mismatch repair (MMR) is characteristic of Lynch syndrome (HNPCC) and can lead to various cancers, particularly colorectal and endometrial cancer. It results in microsatellite instability (MSI). This mechanism is not the primary driver of Burkitt lymphoma, which is characterized by a specific chromosomal translocation involving MYC.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (Endemic) | Follicular Lymphoma | Adult T-cell Leukemia/Lymphoma | Other EBV-associated Lymphomas (e.g., PTLD) |
| :------------------ | :------------------------- | :------------------ | :------------------------------ | :------------------------------------------ |
| **Primary Driver**  | MYC translocation (t(8;14)) | BCL2 translocation (t(14;18)) | HTLV-1 integration, Tax protein | EBV infection, variable genetics            |
| **Morphology**      | "Starry sky", medium cells | Follicular pattern, centrocytes/centroblasts | Mature T-cells, cerebriform nuclei | Variable, often polymorphic                |
| **Immunophenotype** | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+ | CD20+, CD10+, BCL2+, BCL6+ | CD4+, CD25+ (ATL)              | Variable, often CD20+, CD30+                 |
| **Associated Virus**| EBV (strong)               | None                | HTLV-1                         | EBV (common)                                |
| **Key Mechanism**   | MYC overexpression -> uncontrolled proliferation | BCL2 overexpression -> inhibited apoptosis | Tax activation of NF-κB -> proliferation, immortalization | EBV-driven proliferation, variable oncogenesis |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is a high-grade B-cell lymphoma characterized by the t(8;14) translocation leading to MYC overexpression and uncontrolled proliferation. Remember the classic "starry sky" morphology, endemic association with EBV in Africa, and the specific immunophenotype (CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+). Differentiate it from follicular lymphoma (BCL2 translocation, t(14;18)), which involves apoptosis inhibition, and other EBV-associated lymphomas where EBV plays a role but the specific genetic driver is different.

<a id='question-048'></a>

### Question 048: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-48 (° Age groups include adolescents and young adults )
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the characteristic cytogenetic abnormality (Philadelphia chromosome) and its molecular consequence (BCR-ABL fusion protein) in Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML), and differentiating it from other leukemias/lymphomas presenting in young adults. (理解慢性髓性白血病 (CML) 的特征性细胞遗传学异常 (费城染色体) 及其分子后果 (BCR-ABL 融合蛋白)，并将其与其他在年轻人中表现的白血病/淋巴瘤区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and mild abdominal discomfort for the past 3 months. He denies fever, weight loss, or bone pain. Physical examination reveals mild splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows a markedly elevated white blood cell (WBC) count of 180,000/µL (normal range: 4,000-11,000/µL) with a left shift, including numerous myelocytes, metamyelocytes, and bands. Hemoglobin is 11 g/dL (normal range: 13.5-17.5 g/dL), and platelet count is 600,000/µL (normal range: 150,000-450,000/µL). A bone marrow aspirate and biopsy are performed. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the presence of the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome), t(9;22)(q34;q11). Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) confirms the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene.

Which of the following molecular events is the direct consequence of the cytogenetic abnormality described?

(A) Formation of the ETV6-RUNX1 fusion protein, leading to dysregulation of hematopoiesis.
(B) Activation of the JAK-STAT pathway due to constitutive cytokine receptor signaling.
(C) Formation of the BCR-ABL1 fusion protein, resulting in constitutive tyrosine kinase activity.
(D) Overexpression of the BCL2 protein, inhibiting apoptosis of lymphoid cells.
(E) Production of a monoclonal immunoglobulin light chain, leading to plasma cell dyscrasia.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 32-year-old male (young adult).
- **Symptoms:** Fatigue, night sweats, abdominal discomfort (suggestive of systemic illness, possibly hematologic malignancy).
- **Physical Exam:** Mild splenomegaly (common in myeloproliferative neoplasms).
- **CBC:** Markedly elevated WBC count (leukocytosis) with a left shift (immature granulocytes in peripheral blood), mild anemia, and thrombocytosis. This pattern is highly characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML).
- **Bone Marrow Cytogenetics:** Presence of the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome), t(9;22)(q34;q11). This is the hallmark cytogenetic abnormality of CML.
- **FISH:** Confirms the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene, which results from the t(9;22) translocation.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the direct molecular consequence of the described cytogenetic abnormality (t(9;22)).

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome) is a reciprocal translocation between the long arms of chromosomes 9 and 22, denoted as t(9;22)(q34;q11).
- This translocation fuses the ABL1 gene (a proto-oncogene encoding a non-receptor tyrosine kinase) from chromosome 9 to the BCR gene (breakpoint cluster region) on chromosome 22.
- The resulting fusion gene, BCR-ABL1, encodes a novel fusion protein, the BCR-ABL1 tyrosine kinase.
- This BCR-ABL1 protein exhibits constitutive (always "on") tyrosine kinase activity, independent of normal regulatory signals.
- Constitutive tyrosine kinase activity leads to uncontrolled proliferation and survival of myeloid progenitor cells, driving the pathogenesis of CML.
- The BCR-ABL1 protein phosphorylates numerous downstream signaling molecules, activating pathways involved in cell growth, differentiation, and apoptosis inhibition.
- This mechanism is the target for highly effective tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs) like imatinib, dasatinib, and nilotinib, which are the standard of care for CML.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Formation of the ETV6-RUNX1 fusion protein: This fusion protein is characteristic of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL), specifically the ETV6-RUNX1 positive subtype, which is common in childhood ALL. It is not associated with the Philadelphia chromosome or CML.
- (B) Activation of the JAK-STAT pathway due to constitutive cytokine receptor signaling: While the JAK-STAT pathway is often activated in myeloproliferative neoplasms (MPNs), the primary driver in CML is the BCR-ABL1 tyrosine kinase. JAK-STAT activation is more characteristic of conditions like Polycythemia Vera (JAK2 V617F mutation) or Essential Thrombocythemia. Although BCR-ABL can indirectly influence JAK-STAT, the *direct* consequence is the tyrosine kinase activity.
- (D) Overexpression of the BCL2 protein: Overexpression of BCL2, an anti-apoptotic protein, is a hallmark of Follicular Lymphoma, typically resulting from the t(14;18) translocation involving the BCL2 gene. It is not the direct consequence of the Philadelphia chromosome.
- (E) Production of a monoclonal immunoglobulin light chain: This is characteristic of plasma cell dyscrasias, such as Multiple Myeloma or Waldenström Macroglobulinemia, involving the clonal proliferation of plasma cells or lymphoplasmacytic cells producing monoclonal immunoglobulins or light chains. It is unrelated to CML and the Philadelphia chromosome.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature                 | Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) | ETV6-RUNX1 ALL        | JAK2 V617F MPN        | t(14;18) Follicular Lymphoma | Multiple Myeloma        |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------- | :-------------------- | :-------------------- | :-------------------------- | :---------------------- |
| **Age Group**           | Young to Middle-Aged Adults    | Primarily Children    | Middle-Aged/Elderly   | Middle-Aged/Elderly         | Elderly                 |
| **Key Cytogenetic/Gene** | t(9;22) / BCR-ABL1             | t(12;21) / ETV6-RUNX1  | JAK2 V617F mutation   | t(14;18) / BCL2             | Various (e.g., t(11;14)) |
| **Primary Cell Line**   | Myeloid                        | Lymphoid (B-cell)     | Myeloid               | Lymphoid (B-cell)           | Plasma Cells            |
| **Molecular Consequence**| Constitutive Tyrosine Kinase   | Aberrant Transcription| Constitutive Signaling| Anti-apoptotic Protein     | Monoclonal Ig Production|
| **Hallmark Protein**    | BCR-ABL1                       | ETV6-RUNX1            | JAK2 (mutated)        | BCL2                       | Monoclonal Protein     |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The Philadelphia chromosome (t(9;22)) in CML leads directly to the formation of the BCR-ABL1 fusion protein, a constitutively active tyrosine kinase driving myeloid proliferation. Differentiating CML from other leukemias/lymphomas requires recognizing this specific cytogenetic abnormality and its molecular consequence. Key differentials include ALL (ETV6-RUNX1), other MPNs (JAK2 mutations), Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18)/BCL2), and Plasma Cell Dyscrasias (monoclonal proteins).

<a id='question-049'></a>

### Question 049: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-49 (° The “classic triad” includes fever, sore throat )
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the clinical presentation, cytogenetics, and pathophysiology of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL), specifically differentiating it from other hematologic malignancies and recognizing the significance of the Philadelphia chromosome (BCR-ABL1 fusion).

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-year-old boy presents to the pediatrician with a 2-week history of fatigue, pallor, and easy bruising. His mother reports recent frequent nosebleeds and a persistent low-grade fever. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy (cervical, axillary, inguinal), hepatosplenomegaly, and petechiae on his lower extremities. Vital signs show a temperature of 37.8°C (100.0°F), heart rate of 110 bpm, respiratory rate of 24/min, and blood pressure of 95/60 mmHg. Complete blood count (CBC) shows: WBC 85,000/µL (normal 4,500-11,000/µL) with 90% blasts, Hemoglobin 7.5 g/dL (normal 11.5-15.5 g/dL), and Platelets 25,000/µL (normal 150,000-400,000/µL). Bone marrow aspirate and biopsy confirm hypercellularity with 95% lymphoblasts. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the presence of the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome), characterized by the t(9;22)(q34;q11) translocation. Flow cytometry shows the blasts are positive for CD10, CD19, and TdT, but negative for CD15 and CD34. Which of the following molecular abnormalities is most likely responsible for the malignant transformation in this patient?

(A) MYC gene amplification
(B) BCR-ABL1 fusion gene
(C) ETV6-RUNX1 fusion gene
(D) MLL gene rearrangement
(E) NPM1 gene mutation

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 6-year-old boy. This age is typical for childhood ALL.
- **Symptoms:** Fatigue, pallor, easy bruising, nosebleeds, low-grade fever. These are classic symptoms of bone marrow failure (anemia, thrombocytopenia) and potential systemic involvement (fever).
- **Physical Exam:** Generalized lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly, petechiae. These findings suggest infiltration of lymphoid tissues and extramedullary disease, common in leukemia/lymphoma.
- **CBC:** Markedly elevated WBC count (leukocytosis) with a predominance of blasts (90%), severe anemia (low Hb), and severe thrombocytopenia (low platelets). This is characteristic of acute leukemia. The high blast count confirms the diagnosis.
- **Bone Marrow:** Hypercellular with 95% lymphoblasts. Confirms acute lymphoblastic leukemia (ALL).
- **Immunophenotyping (Flow Cytometry):** CD10+, CD19+, TdT+. This profile is characteristic of B-cell lineage ALL (B-ALL). CD15- and CD34- help exclude certain other conditions.
- **Cytogenetics:** Presence of the Philadelphia chromosome (t(9;22)(q34;q11)). This is a specific chromosomal translocation.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular abnormality responsible for malignant transformation *given* the presence of the Philadelphia chromosome.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome) results from a reciprocal translocation between the long arms of chromosomes 9 and 22, denoted as t(9;22)(q34;q11).
- This translocation fuses the *ABL1* gene (Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1) from chromosome 9 with the *BCR* gene (breakpoint cluster region) on chromosome 22.
- The resulting fusion gene, *BCR-ABL1*, encodes a constitutively active tyrosine kinase protein (BCR-ABL).
- This BCR-ABL protein drives uncontrolled proliferation and inhibits apoptosis of hematopoietic progenitor cells, leading to the malignant transformation characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and a subset of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL), particularly in adults but also occurring in children (though less common than other ALL subtypes).
- The question specifically points to the presence of the Ph chromosome, directly linking the molecular abnormality to the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **MYC gene amplification:** MYC is an oncogene involved in cell cycle regulation. Amplification is commonly found in Burkitt lymphoma and some forms of B-ALL, but it is not directly associated with the Philadelphia chromosome.
- (C) **ETV6-RUNX1 fusion gene:** This fusion gene, resulting from the t(12;21)(p13;q22) translocation, is the most common genetic abnormality in childhood B-cell ALL (B-ALL), typically associated with a good prognosis. It is mutually exclusive with the Philadelphia chromosome.
- (D) **MLL gene rearrangement:** MLL (Mixed Lineage Leukemia) gene rearrangements, often involving translocations like t(4;11)(q21;q23) or t(6;11)(q27;q23), are found in infant ALL and some adult ALL subtypes. They are associated with a poor prognosis but are distinct from the Philadelphia chromosome.
- (E) **NPM1 gene mutation:** Mutations in the Nucleophosmin 1 (NPM1) gene are common in acute myeloid leukemia (AML), particularly AML with normal cytogenetics. They are not characteristic of ALL or associated with the Philadelphia chromosome.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature                 | Philadelphia Chromosome (t(9;22)) | ETV6-RUNX1 (t(12;21)) | MLL Rearrangement (e.g., t(4;11)) | MYC Amplification | NPM1 Mutation |
| :---------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :-------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :---------------- | :------------ |
| **Disease Association** | CML, Ph+ ALL                      | Childhood B-ALL       | Infant ALL, Adult ALL             | Burkitt Lymphoma, B-ALL | AML           |
| **Gene Fusion/Mutation** | BCR-ABL1                           | ETV6-RUNX1            | MLL partner gene                  | MYC amplification | NPM1 mutation |
| **Typical Age**         | Adults (CML), Variable (ALL)       | Children             | Infants, Adults                   | Variable          | Adults        |
| **Prognosis (ALL)**     | Historically poor, improved with TKIs | Generally good       | Poor                              | Variable          | Variable      |
| **Immunophenotype**     | B-ALL (most common), CML           | B-ALL                | B-ALL, T-ALL                       | B-ALL             | AML           |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The presence of the Philadelphia chromosome (t(9;22)) in ALL is a critical diagnostic and prognostic marker, directly linked to the formation of the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene and its associated constitutively active tyrosine kinase. Differentiating this from other common genetic abnormalities in ALL (like ETV6-RUNX1, MLL rearrangements) and other malignancies (like MYC amplification in Burkitt lymphoma or NPM1 mutations in AML) is crucial for understanding disease pathogenesis and targeted therapy.

<a id='question-050'></a>

### Question 050: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 01-50 (brane on tonsils, and lymphadenitisinvolving the p)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the characteristic cytogenetic abnormality (translocation) and its resulting fusion protein in Burkitt lymphoma, a high-grade B-cell lymphoma, and its distinction from other lymphomas and leukemias. (本题考察伯基特淋巴瘤的特征性细胞遗传学异常（易位）及其产生的融合蛋白，以及与其他淋巴瘤和白血病的鉴别。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 12-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a rapidly enlarging mass in his jaw, causing facial swelling and difficulty chewing. He also reports intermittent fever and night sweats over the past month. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender mass involving the mandible and extending into the oral cavity. Lymphadenopathy is noted in the cervical region. Laboratory studies show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) levels (1800 U/L, normal <250 U/L) and hyperuricemia (10 mg/dL, normal <7 mg/dL). A biopsy of the jaw mass shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are positive for CD20 and CD10, and negative for CD5 and BCL2. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation involving chromosomes 8 and 14.

(A) A translocation between chromosomes 9 and 22, resulting in the BCR-ABL fusion protein.
(B) A translocation between chromosomes 14 and 18, resulting in the API2-BCL2 fusion protein.
(C) A translocation between chromosomes 8 and 14, resulting in the MYC-IGH fusion protein.
(D) A translocation between chromosomes 11 and 14, resulting in the CCND1-IGH fusion protein.
(E) A translocation between chromosomes 2 and 5, resulting in the ETV6-RUNX1 fusion protein.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 12-year-old boy - Age is typical for endemic Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Presentation:** Rapidly enlarging jaw mass, facial swelling, difficulty chewing, fever, night sweats - Suggests a rapidly proliferating malignancy, often involving extranodal sites like the jaw in endemic Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Physical Exam:** Firm, non-tender jaw mass, cervical lymphadenopathy - Consistent with lymphoma.
- **Labs:** Elevated LDH, hyperuricemia - Indicate high tumor burden and rapid cell turnover, characteristic of high-grade lymphomas like Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Biopsy:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells, round nuclei, clumped chromatin, small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm ("starry sky" appearance often seen but not explicitly mentioned here) - Morphologically consistent with Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+, CD10+, CD5-, BCL2- - Classic immunophenotype for Burkitt lymphoma (B-cell origin, germinal center markers, lack of mantle cell markers, lack of BCL2 overexpression).
- **Cytogenetics:** Translocation involving chromosomes 8 and 14 - This is the hallmark genetic abnormality of Burkitt lymphoma.

The combination of clinical presentation (young patient, jaw mass, rapid growth, high LDH), morphology (medium-sized cells, nucleoli), immunophenotype (CD20+, CD10+, BCL2-), and the specific cytogenetic finding (t(8;14)) strongly points to Burkitt lymphoma. The question asks for the resulting fusion protein from this translocation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Burkitt Lymphoma:** A highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma.
- **Cytogenetics:** The defining feature is a translocation involving the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8 (specifically 8q24) and one of the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) genes on chromosome 14 (specifically 14q32). The most common translocation is t(8;14)(q24;q32). Less common variants involve translocations with the immunoglobulin light chain genes (kappa on chromosome 2 or lambda on chromosome 22), such as t(2;8) or t(8;22).
- **Molecular Mechanism:** The translocation places the *MYC* proto-oncogene under the control of strong immunoglobulin gene enhancers. This leads to constitutive overexpression of the MYC protein, a transcription factor that regulates cell growth, proliferation, apoptosis, and metabolism. Uncontrolled MYC expression drives the rapid proliferation characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Fusion Protein:** The translocation results in a fusion gene, typically *IGH-MYC* in the t(8;14) variant. This fusion gene encodes a chimeric protein containing parts of the IGH protein fused to the MYC protein. While the resulting protein is often referred to as the MYC-IGH fusion protein, the key pathogenic event is the *overexpression* of MYC due to the enhancer elements, rather than the fusion itself conferring a novel function. However, the question asks for the resulting fusion protein.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **t(9;22) resulting in BCR-ABL:** This translocation is characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and some cases of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL). It results in the Philadelphia chromosome and the BCR-ABL fusion protein, a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. This is incorrect because the patient's presentation, morphology, immunophenotype, and the specific translocation t(8;14) are inconsistent with CML/Ph+ ALL.
- (B) **t(14;18) resulting in API2-BCL2:** This translocation is characteristic of Follicular Lymphoma. It places the *BCL2* gene (anti-apoptotic) under the control of the IGH enhancer, leading to BCL2 overexpression and inhibition of apoptosis. This is incorrect because the patient's morphology (diffuse, medium-sized cells) and immunophenotype (BCL2 negative) are inconsistent with Follicular Lymphoma, and the translocation is different.
- (D) **t(11;14) resulting in CCND1-IGH:** This translocation is characteristic of Mantle Cell Lymphoma (MCL). It leads to overexpression of Cyclin D1 (CCND1), promoting cell cycle progression. This is incorrect because the patient's immunophenotype (CD5 negative) is inconsistent with MCL (which is typically CD5 positive), and the translocation is different.
- (E) **t(2;5) resulting in ETV6-RUNX1:** This translocation is characteristic of a subtype of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL), specifically ETV6-RUNX1 positive ALL, often associated with a good prognosis. This is incorrect because the patient has a lymphoma, not ALL, and the translocation is different.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (t(8;14)) | Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18)) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(11;14)) | CML/Ph+ ALL (t(9;22)) | ETV6-RUNX1 ALL (t(2;5)) |
|----------------------|---------------------------|------------------------------|-------------------------------|-----------------------|------------------------|
| **Cell Type**        | B-cell Lymphoma           | B-cell Lymphoma              | B-cell Lymphoma               | Myeloid/Lymphoid Leukemia | Lymphoid Leukemia      |
| **Age**              | Children/Young Adults     | Adults                       | Middle-aged/Elderly            | Adults                | Children               |
| **Clinical**         | Jaw mass, rapid growth    | Generalized lymphadenopathy  | Lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly | Fatigue, splenomegaly | Bone pain, fever       |
| **Morphology**       | Medium cells, starry sky  | Follicular pattern           | Small cells, irregular nuclei  | Myeloid hyperplasia   | Lymphoblasts           |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD20+, CD10+, BCL2-       | CD20+, CD10+, BCL2+           | CD20+, CD5+, Cyclin D1+        | CD34+, Myeloid markers | Pre-B markers          |
| **Cytogenetics**     | t(8;14) or variants       | t(14;18)                     | t(11;14)                      | t(9;22)               | t(2;5)                 |
| **Fusion Protein**   | MYC-IGH                  | API2-BCL2                   | CCND1-IGH                     | BCR-ABL              | ETV6-RUNX1             |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is characterized by the t(8;14) translocation leading to MYC overexpression. Key differentials include other high-grade lymphomas (like Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma) and follicular lymphoma (t(14;18)). Remember the specific translocations and their associated fusion proteins for common lymphomas and leukemias. (伯基特淋巴瘤的特征是t(8;14)易位导致MYC过度表达。关键鉴别包括其他高级别淋巴瘤（如弥漫大B细胞淋巴瘤）和滤泡性淋巴瘤（t(14;18)）。记住常见淋巴瘤和白血病的特定易位及其相关的融合蛋白。)
